Here, the first inequality of (D.1) holds with probability at least  $1 - C \exp(-c\overline{p})$  ( $\overline{p} = \max \{p_1, p_2, p_3\}$ ) by the proof of (Zhang, Cai and Wu, 2018, Theorem 4); while the second inequality holds with probability at least  $1 - \exp(\sum p_k r_k)$  by Lemma E.5. Applying union bound proves the above claim.

Now we start to bound  $\left\| \mathcal{X}^{(0)} - \mathcal{X}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}$  under the condition (D.1). By the initialization procedure, we have

$$
\mathcal {X} ^ {(0)} = \mathcal {Y} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1}} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2}} \times_ {3} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {3}}.
$$

Then it follows that

$$
\left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(0)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1}} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2}} \times_ {3} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {3}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} + \left\| \mathcal {Z} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1}} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2}} \times_ {3} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {3}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}}.
$$

For the first term, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1}} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2}} \times_ {3} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {3}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ = \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1 \perp}} + \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1}} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2 \perp}} + \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1}} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2}} \times_ {3} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {3 \perp}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \leq \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1 \perp}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} + \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2 \perp}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} + \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {3} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {3 \perp}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k \perp} ^ {\top} \mathcal {M} _ {k} \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \end{array}
$$

and for any  $k = 1,2,3$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k \perp} ^ {\top} \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} = \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k \perp} ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {\top} \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \leq \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k \perp} \mathbf {U} _ {k} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {\top} \mathcal {M} _ {k} \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right) \right\| \\ = \left\| \sin \Theta (\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k}, \mathbf {U} _ {k}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \cdot \| \mathcal {M} _ {k} \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right) \| \tag {D.2} \\ \stackrel {\text {(D . 1)}} {\leq} \frac {\underline {{\lambda}} \sqrt {p _ {k} r _ {k}} + \sqrt {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3} r _ {k}}}{\underline {{\lambda}} ^ {2}} \bar {\lambda}. \\ \end{array}
$$

In the mean time, we also have

$$
\left\| \mathcal{Z}\times_{1}\mathbb{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}}\times_{2}\mathbb{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}}\times_{3}\mathbb{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{3}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}} = \sup_{\substack{\mathcal{T}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}},\\ \| \mathcal{T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}}\Big\langle \mathcal{Z},[[\mathcal{T};\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{\top},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{\top}]]\Big\rangle
$$

(D.3)

$$
\stackrel {\text {(D . 1)}} {\leq} C \left(\sqrt {r _ {1} r _ {2} r _ {3}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \sqrt {p _ {k} r _ {k}}\right).
$$

Combining (D.2) and (D.3), we obtain

$$
\left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(0)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq C \left(\kappa^ {2} \frac {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3} \bar {r}}{\underline {{\lambda}} ^ {2}} + \kappa^ {2} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} p _ {k} r _ {k} + r _ {1} r _ {2} r _ {3}\right),
$$

where  $\overline{r} = \max \{r_1,r_2,r_3\}$

Now we start to apply Theorem 3.1. One can verify that the quadratic loss  $L(\mathcal{X}) = \frac{1}{2}\| \mathcal{X} - \mathcal{Y}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}$  satisfies RCG  $(\alpha, \beta, \mathbb{R}^{p_1\times p_2\times p_3})$  with  $\alpha = \beta = 1 / 2$ . Given  $\underline{\lambda}\geq C_0\overline{p}^{3 / 4}\overline{r}^{1 / 4}$  for some sufficiently large  $C_0$  which only depends on  $\kappa$ , we then have  $\| \mathcal{X}^{(0)} - \mathcal{X}^*\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq c\underline{\lambda}^{2} / \kappa^{2}$ , which is the initialization condition required by Theorem 3.1 in the context of sub-Gaussian tensor PCA. Also, by the second inequality in (D.1), we have

$$
\underline {{\lambda}} ^ {2} \geq C \xi^ {2},
$$

and the signal-noise-ratio condition in Theorem 3.1 is also satisfied. In conclusion, we see that, with probability at least  $1 - C \exp (cp)$ , the conditions in Theorem 3.1 are all satisfied, and vanilla gradient descent (Algorithm 1) achieves the following statistical error bound after sufficient iterations:

$$
\left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(T)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq C \xi \leq C \left(\sqrt {r _ {1} r _ {2} r _ {3}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \sqrt {p _ {k} r _ {k}}\right).
$$

Now the proof is finished.

D.2. Proof of Theorem 4.2. By rescaling the overall model by a factor of  $1 / \sqrt{n}$ , we can assume without loss of generality each entry of the design tensor  $\mathcal{A}_i$  comes from i.i.d.  $N(0,1 / n)$  and the noise  $\varepsilon_{i} \sim N(0,\sigma^{2} / n)$ . Recall the loss function is  $L(\mathcal{X}) = \frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n} (\langle \mathcal{A}_i, \mathcal{X} \rangle - y_i)^2$ . Let  $\mathcal{A}: \mathbb{R}^{p_1 \times p_2 \times p_3} \to \mathbb{R}^n$  be the linear operator such that  $[\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{X})]_i = \langle \mathcal{A}_i, \mathcal{X} \rangle$  and  $\mathcal{A}^*$  be the adjoint operator of  $\mathcal{A}$ :

$$
\mathcal {A} ^ {*} (x) = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {n} x _ {j} \mathcal {A} _ {j}, \qquad x \in \mathbb {R} ^ {n}.
$$

Then we can rewrite the model as

$$
y = \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) + \varepsilon \in \mathbb {R} ^ {n}, \quad \varepsilon \sim N (0, (\sigma^ {2} / n) \mathbf {I} _ {n}),
$$

and the loss function can be written as  $L(\mathcal{X}) = \frac{1}{2} \| \mathcal{A}(\mathcal{X}) - y\| _2^2$ .

The proof idea is the same as the proof of Theorem 3.1. However, in tensor regression, we have an analytical form of the gradient:  $\nabla L(\mathcal{X}) = \mathcal{A}^{*}(\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{X}) - y)$ , which helps us to build some tighter results. To make the proof clear and comparable with the proof of Theorem 3.1, we also separate the proof into several steps.

Step 1 (Notations and Conditions) We follow the same notations as we defined in step 1 in the proof of Theorem 3.1. Similarly, we assume the following conditions:

$$
\left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t)} \right\| \leq 1. 0 1 b, \quad \left\| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {S} ^ {(t)}) \right\| \leq \frac {1 . 0 1 \overline {{\lambda}}}{b ^ {3}}, \quad k = 1, 2, 3
$$

$$
E ^ {(t)} \leq c _ {0} \frac {\lambda^ {1 / 2}}{\kappa^ {3 / 2}}, \quad t = 1, \dots , t _ {\max }
$$

where  $c_{0}$  is some absolute constant we will specify later. Besides, we impose the following high-probability conditions:

$(A_{1})$

(D.6)

$$
\xi := \sup_{\substack{\mathcal{T}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}},\\ \operatorname {rank}(\mathcal{T})\leq (r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}),\\ \| \mathcal{T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}}\langle \nabla L(\mathcal{X}^{*}),\mathcal{T}\rangle = \sup_{\substack{\mathcal{T}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}},\\ \operatorname {rank}(\mathcal{T})\leq (r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}),\\ \| \mathcal{T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}}\langle \mathcal{A}^{*}(\varepsilon),\mathcal{T}\rangle \leq 2\sigma \sqrt{\frac{df}{n}},
$$

where  $df \coloneqq r_1r_2r_3 + \sum_{k=1}^{3}p_kr_k$ .

$(A_{2})$  For any tensor  $\mathcal{X}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_1\times p_2\times p_3}$  such that  $\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{X})\leq (3r_1,3r_2,3r_3)$

$$
\frac {9}{1 0} \| \mathcal {X} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq \| \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X}) \| _ {2} \leq \frac {1 1}{9} \| \mathcal {X} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2},
$$

$$
\left\| \mathcal {A} ^ {*} (\mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X})) \right\| _ {2} \leq \frac {4}{3} \left(\sqrt {\frac {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}}{n}} \vee 1\right) \| \mathcal {X} \| _ {\mathrm {F}}. \tag {D.7}
$$

We will verify the validity of these assumptions at the end of the proof. Also, we assume  $b = \overline{\lambda}^{1/4}$  to simplify the proof.

Step 2 In this step, we exactly follows the proof of (C.5) in Theorem 3.1 and obtain

$$
E ^ {(t + 1)} \leq E ^ {(t)} - 2 \eta \left(Q _ {\mathcal {S}, 1} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} Q _ {k, 1}\right) + \eta^ {2} \left(Q _ {\mathcal {S}, 2} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} Q _ {k, 2}\right), \tag {D.8}
$$

where

$$
Q _ {k, 1} = \left\langle \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} _ {k} ^ {(t)}, \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)}) \right\rangle + \frac {a}{4} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t) \top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t)} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} _ {r _ {k}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} - \frac {c _ {0}}{4} a \sqrt {\underline {{\lambda}}} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t)} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} ^ {(t)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2},
$$

$$
Q _ {k, 2} = 8 \overline {{\lambda}} ^ {2} b ^ {- 2} \left(\xi^ {2} + \left\| \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)}) - \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}\right) + \frac {5}{2} a ^ {2} b ^ {2} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t) \top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t)} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} _ {r _ {k}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2},
$$

$$
Q _ {\mathcal {S}, 1} = \left\langle \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} _ {\mathcal {S}} ^ {(t)}, \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)}) \right\rangle ,
$$

$$
Q _ {\mathcal {S}, 2} = 4 b ^ {6} \left(\xi^ {2} + \left\| \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)}) - \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}\right).
$$

Step 3 In this step, we provide a sharper lower bound for  $Q_{\mathcal{S},1} + \sum_{k=1}^{3} Q_{k,1}$ :

$$
Q _ {\mathcal {S}, 1} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} Q _ {k, 1}
$$

(D.9)

$$
\geq \rho \frac {\lambda^ {3 / 2}}{\kappa^ {1 / 2}} E ^ {(t)} + \frac {a}{8} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t) \top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t)} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} _ {r _ {k}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2 0} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} - C _ {1} \kappa^ {2} \xi^ {2},
$$

where  $\rho$  is some universal small constant and  $C_1$  is some universal big constant. First of all, by the proof of Lemma E.3, we have

$$
4 \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} _ {\mathcal {S}} ^ {(t)} - \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {X} _ {k} ^ {(t)} = \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} + \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon},
$$

where

$$
\mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} = \llbracket S ^ {*}; \mathbf {H} _ {1}, \mathbf {H} _ {2}, \mathbf {H} _ {3} \rrbracket + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} S ^ {*} \times_ {k} \mathbf {U} _ {k} \mathbf {R} _ {k} ^ {\top} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 1} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 2}
$$

(D.10)

$$
+ \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} \times_ {k} \mathbf {H} _ {k} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 1} ^ {\top} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 2} ^ {\top},
$$

$$
\mathbf {H} _ {k} := \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} \mathbf {R} _ {k} ^ {\top}, \quad k = 1, 2, 3,
$$

$$
\mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} := \mathcal {S} ^ {*} - \llbracket \mathcal {S}; \mathbf {R} _ {1}, \mathbf {R} _ {2}, \mathbf {R} _ {3} \rrbracket .
$$

Then it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\langle 4 \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} _ {\mathcal {S}} ^ {(t)} - \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {X} _ {k} ^ {(t)}, \nabla L \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)}\right) \right\rangle = \left\langle \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*}, \nabla L \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)}\right) - \nabla L \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right) \right\rangle \tag {D.11} \\ \left. \right. + \left\langle \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon}, \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)}) - \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\rangle + \left\langle 4 \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} _ {\mathcal {S}} ^ {(t)} - \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {X} _ {k} ^ {(t)}, \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\rangle . \\ \end{array}
$$

Since  $\nabla L(\mathcal{X}^{(t)}) - \nabla L(\mathcal{X}^{*}) = \mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{X}^{(t)} - \mathcal{X}^{*})$ , we firstly have

(D.12)

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\langle \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*}, \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)}) - \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\rangle = \left\langle \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*}, \mathcal {A} ^ {*} \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\rangle \\ = \left\langle \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*}), \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\rangle = \left\| \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \overset {\mathrm {(D . 7)}} {\geq} \frac {4}{5} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Next we give an upper bound of  $\left|\left\langle \mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}, \nabla L(\mathcal{X}^{(t)}) - \nabla L(\mathcal{X}^{*}) \right\rangle \right|$ . To this end, we need to use the fact that  $\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}$  is a summation of rank-  $(r_1, r_2, r_3)$  tensors. By Lemma E.7, for any rank-  $(r_1, r_2, r_3)$  tensor  $\mathcal{X}' \in \mathbb{R}^{p_1 \times p_2 \times p_3}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left\langle \mathcal {X} ^ {\prime}, \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)}) - \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\rangle \right| = \left| \left\langle \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X} ^ {\prime}), \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\rangle \right| \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(D . 7)}} {\leq} \left| \left\langle \mathcal {X} ^ {\prime}, \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\rangle \right| + \frac {1}{2} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq \frac {3}{2} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Thus by plugging each component in the right hand side of (D.10) in  $\mathcal{X}'$  and using triangle inequality, we obtain the following upper bound:

$$
\left| \left\langle \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon}, \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)}) - \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\rangle \right| \leq \frac {3}{2} C _ {\Delta} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}},
$$

where  $C_{\Delta}$  is defined as

$$
\begin{array}{l} C _ {\Delta} := \left\| \llbracket \mathcal {S} ^ {*}; \mathbf {H} _ {1}, \mathbf {H} _ {2}, \mathbf {H} _ {3} \rrbracket \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathcal {S} ^ {*} \times_ {k} \mathbf {U} _ {k} \mathbf {R} _ {k} ^ {\top} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 1} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} \times_ {k} \mathbf {H} _ {k} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 1} ^ {\top} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 2} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

By the proof of Lemma E.3, we know that

$$
\begin{array}{l} C _ {\Delta} \stackrel {(\mathrm {D . 4})} {\leq} 1. 0 1 \bar {\lambda} b ^ {- 3} (E ^ {(t)}) ^ {3 / 2} + 3 (1. 0 1 b) ^ {2} E ^ {(t)} + 3 \cdot \frac {1 . 0 1 \bar {\lambda}}{b ^ {3}} \cdot (1. 0 1 b) E ^ {(t)} \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(D . 5)}} {\leq} \left(1. 0 1 c _ {0} ^ {1 / 2} \bar {\lambda} \underline {{\lambda}} ^ {1 / 4} b ^ {- 3} + 6 \cdot 1. 0 1 ^ {2} b ^ {2}\right) E ^ {(t)} \\ \leq 6. 5 \kappa^ {1 / 2} \underline {{\lambda}} ^ {1 / 2} E ^ {(t)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Note that the above inequality is similar to (C.16) in the proof of Theorem 3.1. Combining all above, we obtain:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left\langle \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon}, \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)}) - \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\rangle \right| \leq \frac {3}{2} C _ {\Delta} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \leq 1 0 \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \cdot \left(\kappa^ {1 / 2} \underline {{\lambda}} ^ {1 / 2} E ^ {(t)}\right) \\ \leq \frac {1}{4} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + 1 0 0 \kappa \underline {{\lambda}} (E ^ {(t)}) ^ {2} \tag {D.13} \\ \stackrel {(D. 5)} {\leq} \frac {1}{4} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {F} ^ {2} + \frac {1 0 0 c _ {0} \underline {{\lambda}} ^ {3 / 2}}{\kappa^ {1 / 2}} E ^ {(t)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

In the meantime, by the same argument in (C.17) in the proof of Theorem 3.1, we can show that

$$
\left| \left\langle 4 \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} _ {\mathcal {S}} ^ {(t)} - \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {X} _ {k} ^ {(t)}, \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\rangle \right| \leq c _ {1} \frac {\lambda^ {3 / 2}}{\kappa^ {1 / 2}} E ^ {(t)} + \frac {8 1 \kappa^ {2}}{4 c _ {1}} \xi^ {2}, \tag {D.14}
$$

and  $c_{1}$  is some universal constant which will be specified later. Now combining (D.11), (D.12), (D.13) and (D.14) and specifying  $a = \frac{2b^4}{3\kappa^2}$  (which is similar to (C.20)), we obtain that

$$
\begin{array}{l} Q _ {\mathcal {S}, 1} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} Q _ {k, 1} \geq \frac {1}{2} \left(\left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{6} \kappa^ {- 2} b ^ {4} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t) \top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t)} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} _ {r _ {k}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \frac {a}{8} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t) \top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t)} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} _ {r _ {k}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} - \frac {c _ {0} \underline {{\lambda}} ^ {3 / 2}}{6 \kappa^ {1 / 2}} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t)} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} ^ {(t)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ + \frac {1}{2 0} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} - \frac {(1 0 0 c _ {0} + c _ {1}) \underline {{\lambda}} ^ {3 / 2}}{\kappa^ {1 / 2}} E ^ {(t)} - C _ {1} \kappa^ {2} \xi^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \geq \frac {\lambda^ {3 / 2}}{9 6 0 \kappa^ {1 / 2}} E ^ {(t)} + \frac {a}{8} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t) \top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t)} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} _ {r _ {k}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2 0} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ - \frac {(1 0 0 c _ {0} + c _ {2}) \underline {{\lambda}} ^ {3 / 2}}{\kappa^ {1 / 2}} E ^ {(t)} - C _ {1} \kappa^ {2} \xi^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
= \rho \frac {\lambda^ {3 / 2}}{\kappa^ {1 / 2}} E ^ {(t)} + \frac {a}{8} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t) \top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t)} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} _ {r _ {k}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2 0} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} - C _ {1} \kappa^ {2} \xi^ {2},
$$

Here in the last step, we specify  $c_0$  and  $c_1$  to be small constants such that  $\rho := \frac{1}{960} - 100c_0 - c_1 > 0$ , and  $C_1 = \frac{81}{4c_1}$ , which gives (D.9).

Step 4 In this step, we provide the error contraction of  $E^{(t)}$ . By plugging in  $a = \frac{2b^4}{3\kappa^2}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} Q _ {\mathcal {S}, 2} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} Q _ {k, 2} \\ \leq 1 2 b ^ {6} \left\| \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)}) - \nabla L (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + \frac {2 b ^ {1 0}}{\kappa^ {4}} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t) \top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t)} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} _ {r _ {k}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + 1 2 b ^ {6} \xi^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\left\| \nabla L \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)}\right) - \nabla L \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} = \left\| \mathcal {A} ^ {*} \mathcal {A} \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq \frac {1 6}{9} \left(\frac {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}}{n} \vee 1\right) \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}.
$$

Now by taking  $\eta = \eta_0 / b^6$  in (D.8) and applying (D.9), we have

(D.15)

$$
\begin{array}{l} E ^ {(t + 1)} \leq \left(1 - \frac {2 \rho \eta_ {0}}{\kappa^ {2}}\right) E ^ {(t)} - \left(\frac {1}{1 0 b ^ {6}} \eta_ {0} - \frac {6 4}{3 b ^ {6}} \left(\frac {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}}{n} \vee 1\right) \eta_ {0} ^ {2}\right) \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ - \left(\frac {b ^ {- 2}}{1 2 \kappa^ {2}} \eta_ {0} - \frac {2 b ^ {- 2}}{\kappa^ {4}} \eta_ {0} ^ {2}\right) \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t) \top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {(t)} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} _ {r _ {k}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + 2 C _ {1} b ^ {- 6} \kappa^ {2} \eta_ {0} \xi^ {2} + 1 2 b ^ {- 6} \eta_ {0} ^ {2} \xi^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then as long as  $\eta_0 < \frac{3}{640}\left(\frac{n}{p_1p_2p_3} \wedge 1\right)$ ,

$$
\frac {1}{1 0 b ^ {6}} \eta_ {0} - \frac {6 4}{3 b ^ {6}} \left(\frac {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}}{n} \vee 1\right) \eta_ {0} ^ {2} > 0,
$$

$$
\frac {b ^ {- 2}}{1 2 \kappa^ {2}} \eta_ {0} - \frac {2 b ^ {- 2}}{\kappa^ {4}} \eta_ {0} ^ {2} > 0,
$$

and it follows that

$$
E ^ {(t + 1)} \leq \left(1 - \frac {2 \rho \eta_ {0}}{\kappa^ {2}}\right) E ^ {(t)} + (2 C _ {1} + 1) \kappa^ {2} b ^ {- 6} \eta_ {0} \xi^ {2}. \tag {D.16}
$$

By induction (as we proved for (C.24)), we can then show that

$$
\begin{array}{l} E ^ {(t)} \leq \frac {\left(2 C _ {1} + 1\right) \kappa^ {4} \xi^ {2}}{2 \rho b ^ {6}} + \left(1 - \frac {2 \rho \eta_ {0}}{\kappa^ {2}}\right) ^ {t} E ^ {(0)} \tag {D.17} \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(D . 6)}} {\leq} C \kappa^ {4} \frac {\sigma^ {2} \cdot d f}{n b ^ {6}} + \left(1 - \frac {2 \rho \eta_ {0}}{\kappa^ {2}}\right) ^ {t} E ^ {(0)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Applying Lemma E.2, we then have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(t)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq 4 2 b ^ {6} E ^ {(t)} \leq C \kappa^ {4} \frac {\sigma^ {2} \cdot d f}{n} + 4 2 \left(1 - \frac {2 \rho \eta_ {0}}{\kappa^ {2}}\right) ^ {t} E ^ {(0)}. \\ \leq C \left(\kappa^ {4} \frac {\sigma^ {2} \cdot d f}{n} + \left(1 - \frac {2 \rho \eta_ {0}}{\kappa^ {2}}\right) ^ {t} \kappa^ {2} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(0)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, for sufficiently large  $T$ , one can see that we have  $\left\| \mathcal{X}^{(T)} - \mathcal{X}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2} \leq C\kappa^{4}\frac{\sigma^{2}\cdot df}{n}$ . Step 5 Finally, we check the validity of conditions. We first assume the conditions (D.6), (D.7) hold, and verify (D.4) and (D.5); then we show that (D.6) and (D.7) hold with high probability. We start from (D.5). By the proof of (Zhang et al., 2019, Theorem 4), we know as long as

$$
n \geq C \frac {\left(\| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + \sigma^ {2}\right) \left(\bar {p} ^ {3 / 2} + \kappa \bar {p} \cdot \bar {r}\right)}{\underline {{\lambda}} ^ {2}},
$$

the initialization estimator has the following error bound:

$$
\left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(0)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq C ^ {\prime} \frac {d f (\sigma^ {2} + \| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2})}{n} \leq C ^ {\prime} \frac {d f \cdot \| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}}{n},
$$

for some universal constants  $C, C'$ . Then by Lemma E.2, we have

$$
E ^ {(0)} \leq 1 1 \kappa^ {2} b ^ {- 6} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(0)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq \frac {C \kappa^ {2} d f \cdot \overline {{r}} \overline {{\lambda}} ^ {2}}{n \overline {{\lambda}} ^ {3 / 2}} \leq c _ {0} \frac {\underline {{\lambda}} ^ {1 / 2}}{\kappa^ {3 / 2}}.
$$

Here we use the assumption that

$$
n \geq C \kappa^ {4} d f \cdot \overline {{r}}.
$$

Thus we show that (D.5) holds at  $t = 0$ . Now suppose (D.5) holds at  $t$ , we show it also holds at  $t + 1$ . By (D.16),

$$
\begin{array}{l} E ^ {(t + 1)} \leq \left(1 - \frac {2 \rho \eta_ {0}}{\kappa^ {2}}\right) E ^ {(t)} + (2 C _ {1} + 1) \kappa^ {2} b ^ {- 6} \eta_ {0} \xi^ {2} \\ \leq \left(1 - \frac {2 \rho \eta_ {0}}{\kappa^ {2}}\right) c _ {0} \frac {\lambda^ {1 / 2}}{\kappa^ {3 / 2}} + (2 C _ {1} + 1) \kappa^ {2} b ^ {- 6} \eta_ {0} \xi^ {2} \\ = c _ {0} \frac {\underline {{\lambda}} ^ {1 / 2}}{\kappa^ {3 / 2}} - \left(\frac {2 c _ {0} \rho \underline {{\lambda}} ^ {1 / 2}}{\kappa^ {3 . 5}} - (2 C _ {1} + 1) \kappa^ {1 / 2} \underline {{\lambda}} ^ {- 3 / 2} \xi^ {2}\right) \eta_ {0} \\ \leq c _ {0} \frac {\lambda^ {1 / 2}}{\kappa^ {3 / 2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Here in the last inequality we use the signal-noise-ratio assumption:  $\underline{\lambda}^2 \geq C\kappa^4\frac{df\sigma^2}{n}$ . Thus (D.5) is verified for any  $t = 0,\dots,t_{max}$ . The verification of (D.4) is the same as we did for (C.3), and we omitted it here. Now we start to show that (D.6), (D.7) hold with high probability. Since  $n \geq Cdf$ , we know that (D.7) holds with probability at least  $1 - C\exp(-c\cdot df)$  by Lemma E.6.

On the other hand, by definition,

$$
\xi := \sup_{\substack{\mathcal{S}\in \mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\| \mathcal{S}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1\\ \mathbf{W}_{k}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times r_{1}},\| \mathbf{W}_{k}\| \leq 1,k = 1,2,3}}\langle \mathcal{A}^{*}(\varepsilon),[[\mathcal{S};\mathbf{W}_{1},\mathbf{W}_{2},\mathbf{W}_{3}]]\rangle .
$$

Notice that for any fixed  $S, W_1, \mathbf{W}_2, \mathbf{W}_3$ , one have

$$
\langle \mathcal {A} ^ {*} (\varepsilon), [   [ S; \mathbf {W} _ {1}, \mathbf {W} _ {2}, \mathbf {W} _ {3} ]   ] \rangle = \langle \varepsilon , \mathcal {A} ([   [ S; \mathbf {W} _ {1}, \mathbf {W} _ {2}, \mathbf {W} _ {3} ]   ]) \rangle ,
$$

which has normal distribution with mean zero and variance  $\frac{\tau^2\sigma^2}{n}$  when conditional on  $\{\mathcal{A}_i\}_{i=1}^n$ , with  $\tau := \|\mathcal{A}([\mathcal{S}; \mathbf{W}_1, \mathbf{W}_2, \mathbf{W}_3])\|_2$ . Thus we have

$$
\mathbb {P} \big (\langle \mathcal {A} ^ {*} (\varepsilon), [ [ \mathcal {S}; \mathbf {W} _ {1}, \mathbf {W} _ {2}, \mathbf {W} _ {3} ] ] \rangle \geq t | \{\mathcal {A} _ {i} \} _ {i = 1} ^ {n} \big) \leq e ^ {- \frac {n t ^ {2}}{2 \tau^ {2} \sigma^ {2}}}.
$$

Under the event  $A \coloneqq \{(\mathrm{D}.7)$  holds\},  $\tau^2 \leq \frac{3}{2} \| [[S; \mathbf{W}_1, \mathbf{W}_2, \mathbf{W}_3]] \|_{\mathrm{F}}^2 \leq \frac{3}{2}$ , then it follows that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\langle \mathcal {A} ^ {*} (\varepsilon), [   [ S; \mathbf {W} _ {1}, \mathbf {W} _ {2}, \mathbf {W} _ {3} ]   ] \rangle \geq t | A\right) \leq e ^ {- \frac {n t ^ {2}}{3 \sigma^ {2}}}. \tag {D.18}
$$

Now for  $k = 1,2,3$ , we can construct an  $\varepsilon$ -net  $\left\{\mathbf{V}_k^{(1)},\ldots ,\mathbf{V}_k^{(N_k)}\right\}$  of Stiefel  $\mathbb{O}_{p_k,r_k}$  with metric  $d(\mathbf{V}_1,\mathbf{V}_2) = \left\| \mathbf{V}_1\mathbf{V}_1^\top -\mathbf{V}_2\mathbf{V}_2^\top \right\|$ , such that

$$
\sup  _ {\mathbf {V} _ {k} \in \mathbb {O} _ {p _ {k}, r _ {k}}} \min  _ {i \leq N _ {k}} d (\mathbf {V} _ {k}, \mathbf {V} _ {k} ^ {(i)}) \leq \varepsilon
$$

and  $N_{k}\leq \left(\frac{c_{0}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{p_{k}(p_{k} - r_{k})}$  for some absolute constant  $c_{0}$ . Also, we can construct  $\varepsilon$ -net  $\{\mathcal{S}^{(1)},\ldots ,\mathcal{S}^{(N_S)}\}$  for the core tensors on  $\mathbb{B}_{r_1,r_2,r_3}$ , which is the  $l_{2}$  unit ball for  $\mathbb{R}^{r_1\times r_2\times r_3}$ , such that

$$
\sup _ {\mathcal {S} \in \mathbb {B} _ {r _ {1}, r _ {2}, r _ {3}}} \min _ {i \leq N _ {\mathcal {S}}} \left\| \mathcal {S} - \mathcal {S} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq \varepsilon
$$

and  $N_{\mathcal{S}} \leq \left(\frac{c_1}{\varepsilon}\right)^{r_1 r_2 r_3}$ . Then by the similar  $\varepsilon$ -net argument in Lemma E.5, we can show that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\xi > 2 \sqrt {\frac {\sigma^ {2} d f}{n}} \Bigg | A\right) \leq C \exp (- c \cdot d f)),
$$

and it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\xi <   2 \sqrt {\frac {\sigma^ {2} d f}{n}}\right) \geq \mathbb {P} (A) \cdot \mathbb {P} \left(N _ {1} > C \sqrt {\frac {\sigma^ {2} d f}{n}} \Bigg | A\right) \\ \geq \left(1 - C e ^ {- c \cdot d f}\right) \left(1 - C e ^ {- c \cdot d f}\right) \geq 1 - 2 C e ^ {- c \cdot d f}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Thus we proved that with probability at least  $1 - Ce^{-c \cdot df}$ , (D.7) holds, now the proof is finished.

D.3. Proof of Theorem 4.3. We first introduce some notations to simplify the proof. Recall for each  $(j,k,l)\in [p_1]\times [p_2]\times [p_3],\mathcal{Y}_{jkl}\sim \mathrm{Poisson}(\nu_{jkl})$ , where  $\nu_{jkl}\coloneqq I\exp (\mathcal{X}_{jkl}^{*})$ . By Assumption 4.1, we have  $|\mathcal{X}_{jkl}^{*}|\leq B$ . We next define a new random tensor  $\mathcal{V}'$  such that  $\mathcal{V}_{jkl}' = \mathcal{V}_{jkl}1_{\{\frac{\nu_{jkl}}{10}\leq \mathcal{Y}_{jkl}\leq 10\nu_{jkl}\}} + \nu_{jkl}1_{\{\mathcal{Y}_{jkl}\notin [\frac{\nu_{jkl}}{10},10\nu_{jkl}]\}}$ , and we further define  $\mathcal{Z}\coloneqq \mathcal{Z}_1+$ $\mathcal{Z}_2$  where

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {Z} _ {1} = \log \left(\mathcal {Y} ^ {\prime} + 1 / 2\right) - \mathbb {E} \log \left(\mathcal {Y} ^ {\prime} + 1 / 2\right), \\ \mathcal {Z} _ {2} = \mathbb {E} \log \left(\mathcal {Y} ^ {\prime} + 1 / 2\right) - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} - \log I. \\ \end{array}
$$

The following conditions are introduced for the proof:

$(A_{1})$

(D.19)  $\mathcal{V}_{jkl} = \mathcal{V}_{jkl}^{\prime},\quad \forall (j,k,l)\in [p_1]\times [p_2]\times [p_3]$

$(A_{2})$

(D.20)  $\| \mathcal{M}_k(\mathcal{Z}_1)\| \leq 2K_0\sqrt{\frac{e^B}{I}}\left(\sqrt{p_{-k}} +\sqrt{p_k}\right),\quad k = 1,2,3$

$(A_{3})$

(D.21)  $\sup_{\| \mathbf{V}_k\| \leq 1,k = 1,2,3}\left\| \mathcal{Z}_1\times_1\mathbf{V}_1^\top \times_2\mathbf{V}_2^\top \times_3\mathbf{V}_3^\top \right\| _F\leq C_1\frac{\sqrt{df}}{\sqrt{I / e^B}},$

$(A_4)$

(D.22)  $\sup_{\substack{\mathcal{T}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_1\times p_2\times p_3},\\ \operatorname {rank}(\mathcal{T})\leq (r_1,r_2,r_3),\\ \| \mathcal{T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}}\langle \mathcal{Y} / I - \exp (\mathcal{X}^{\ast}),\mathcal{T}\rangle \leq C_2\frac{\sqrt{df}}{\sqrt{I / e^B}}.$

Here  $K_0, C_1, C_2$  are some universal constants and  $df \coloneqq r_1r_2r_3 + \sum_{k=1}^{3}p_kr_k$ . We now start the analysis assuming that the above conditions hold, and we will verify that they hold with high probability at last.

First of all, by Lemma E.9, if we set  $I \geq Ce^{B}$ , then for each  $(j,k,l) \in [p_1] \times [p_2] \times [p_3]$ , we have

$\left|\mathbb{E}\log (\mathcal{Y}_{jkl}^{\prime} + 1 / 2) - \mathcal{X}_{jkl}^{*} - \log I\right|\leq 4\nu_{jkl}^{-1}\leq \frac{4e^{B}}{I},$  (D.23)

$$
\left\| \log (\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} + 1 / 2) - \mathbb {E} \log (\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} + 1 / 2) \right\| _ {\psi_ {2}} \leq K _ {0} \nu_ {j k l} ^ {- 1 / 2} \leq K _ {0} \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {B}}{I}},
$$

where  $K_{0}$  is the constant defined in Lemma E.9. Now we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {Z} _ {2}) \right\| \leq \left\| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {Z} _ {2}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ = \sqrt {\sum_ {j k l} \left| \mathbb {E} \log \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} + 1 / 2\right) - \mathcal {X} _ {j k l} ^ {*} - \log I \right| ^ {2}} \stackrel {\text {(D . 2 3)}} {\leq} \sqrt {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3} \left(\frac {4 e ^ {B}}{I}\right) ^ {2}} = \frac {4 e ^ {B} \sqrt {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}}}{I}, \\ \end{array}
$$

and then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {Z}) \| \leq \| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {Z} _ {1}) \| + \| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {Z} _ {2}) \| \\ \stackrel {\text {(D . 2 0)}} {\leq} 2 K _ {0} \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {B}}{I}} \left(\sqrt {p _ {- k}} + \sqrt {p _ {k}}\right) + \frac {4 e ^ {B} \sqrt {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}}}{I} \leq C \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {B}}{I}} \left(\sqrt {p _ {- k}} + \sqrt {p _ {k}}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality comes from the assumption that  $I > C\overline{p}e^{B}$ , and  $\overline{p} = \max \{p_1, p_2, p_3\}$ . Since  $\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_k^{(0)}$  is the  $r_k$  leading singular vectors of  $\mathcal{M}_k(\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)})$ , and  $\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)} = \log ((\mathcal{Y} + 1/2)/I) \stackrel{(D.19)}{=} \log ((\mathcal{Y}' + 1/2)/I) = \mathcal{X}^* + \mathcal{Z}$ , it follows that

$$
\left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k \perp} ^ {(0) \top} \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \stackrel {\text {L e m m a E . 4}} {\leq} 2 \sqrt {r _ {k}} \| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {Z}) \| \leq C \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {B}}{I}} \left(\sqrt {p _ {- k} r _ {k}} + \sqrt {p _ {k} r _ {k}}\right).
$$

Here  $\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{(0)}\in \mathbb{O}_{p_k,p_k - r_k}$  and is orthogonal to  $\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_k$ . Now we can obtain the upper bound of  $\left\| \mathcal{X}^{(0)} - \mathcal{X}^*\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^2$ . Since

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(0)}} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2} ^ {(0)}} \times_ {3} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {3} ^ {(0)}} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ = \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1 \perp} ^ {(0)}} + \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(0)}} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2 \perp} ^ {(0)}} + \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(0)}} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2} ^ {(0)}} \times_ {3} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {3 \perp} ^ {(0)}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \leq \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {k} P _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k \perp} ^ {(0)}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k \perp} ^ {(0) \top} \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq C \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {B}}{I}} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left(\sqrt {p _ {- k} r _ {k}} + \sqrt {p _ {k} r _ {k}}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(0)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} = \left\| \tilde {\mathcal {X}} ^ {(0)} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(0)}} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2} ^ {(0)}} \times_ {3} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {3} ^ {(0)}} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \leq \left\| \mathcal {Z} _ {1} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(0)}} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2} ^ {(0)}} \times_ {3} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {3} ^ {(0)}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} + \left\| \mathcal {Z} _ {2} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(0)}} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2} ^ {(0)}} \times_ {3} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {3} ^ {(0)}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {I}} \\ + \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \times_ {1} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(0)}} \times_ {2} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2} ^ {(0)}} \times_ {3} \mathbb {P} _ {\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {3} ^ {(0)}} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {\mathrm {(D . 2 1)}} {\leq} C \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {B}}{I}} \left(\sqrt {r _ {1} r _ {2} r _ {3}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \sqrt {p _ {k} r _ {k}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \sqrt {p _ {- k} r _ {k}}\right) + \| \mathcal {Z} _ {2} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \leq C \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {B}}{I}} \left(\sqrt {r _ {1} r _ {2} r _ {3}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \sqrt {p _ {k} r _ {k}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \sqrt {p _ {- k} r _ {k}}\right) + \frac {4 e ^ {B} \sqrt {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}}}{I} \\ \leq C \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {B}}{I}} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left(\sqrt {p _ {k} r _ {k}} + \sqrt {p _ {- k} r _ {k}}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality comes from the assumption that  $I > e^{B}\overline{p}$ .

Now we turn to apply Theorem 3.1. We take the loss function to be the normalized Poisson negative log-likelihood

$$
L (\mathcal {X}) = \frac {1}{I} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {p _ {1}} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {p _ {2}} \sum_ {l = 1} ^ {p _ {3}} \left(- \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} \mathcal {X} _ {j k l} + I \exp \left(\mathcal {X} _ {i j k}\right)\right)
$$

with  $\nabla L(\mathcal{X}) = -\mathcal{Y} / I + \exp (\mathcal{X}^*)$ . In the convex region  $\{\mathcal{X}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_1\times p_2\times p_3}:\| \mathcal{X}\|_{\infty}\leq B\}$ ,  $L(\mathcal{X})$  is  $e^{B}$ -smooth and  $e^{-B}$ -strongly convex, and by Lemma E.1, we know  $L(\mathcal{X})$  satisfies  $\operatorname{RCG}(\alpha ,\beta ,\mathcal{C})$  with  $\alpha = \beta = \frac{1}{e^{B} + e^{-B}}$  and  $\mathcal{C}$  is defined according to (3.3) and (4.6). Then as long as  $I\underline{\lambda}^2\geq C\kappa^2 e^{3B}\sum_{k = 1}^3 (p_{-k}r_k + p_kr_k)$  for some universal constant  $C$ , the initialization condition  $\left\| \mathcal{X}^{(0)} - \mathcal{X}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq c_{0}\alpha \beta \kappa^{-2}\underline{\lambda}^{2}$  in Theorem 3.1 is satisfied. In the meantime, one can calculate that

$$
\xi = \sup_{\substack{\mathcal{T}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}},\\ \operatorname {rank}(\mathcal{T})\leq (r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}),\\ \| \mathcal{T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}}\langle \mathcal{Y} / I - \exp (\mathcal{X}^{*}),\mathcal{T}\rangle \overset {\text{(D.22)}}{\leq}C\frac{\sqrt{df}}{\sqrt{I / e^{B}}}.
$$

Then  $\underline{\lambda}^2 \geq C\kappa^4 e^{5B}\left(r_1r_2r_3 + \sum_{k=1}^{3}p_kr_k\right)$  implies the signal-noise-ratio condition  $\underline{\lambda}^2 \geq C\frac{\kappa^4}{\alpha^3\beta}\xi^2$  in Theorem 3.1, and we can obtain the statistical error rate of gradient descent after sufficient steps:

$$
\left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(T)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq C \frac {\kappa^ {4}}{\alpha^ {2}} \xi^ {2} \leq \frac {C \kappa^ {4} e ^ {3 B}}{I} \cdot d f.
$$

Now it suffices to check (D.19)-(D.22) actually hold with high probability. First of all, let  $A$  be the event that  $\mathcal{Y}_{jkl}^{\prime} = \mathcal{Y}_{jkl}, \forall j, k, l$ , then we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} (A) = 1 - \mathbb {P} \left(\exists (j, k, l), \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} \notin [ \frac {\nu_ {j k l}}{1 0}, 1 0 \nu_ {j k l} ]\right) \\ \geq 1 - \sum_ {j, k, l} \left(\mathbb {P} (\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} <   \frac {\nu_ {j k l}}{1 0}) + \mathbb {P} (\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} > 1 0 \nu_ {j k l})\right) \\ \stackrel {(a)} {\geq} 1 - 2 \sum_ {j, k, l} \exp \left(- \frac {4}{5} \nu_ {j k l}\right) \geq 1 - 2 \sum_ {j k l} \exp \left(- \frac {2}{5} I e ^ {- B}\right) \\ = 1 - 2 \exp \left(\log (p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}) - \frac {2 e ^ {- B}}{5} I\right) \stackrel {(b)} {\geq} 1 - \frac {C}{p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Here (a) comes from applying Poisson's tail bound (Lemma E.8) and (b) is true as long as  $I > Ce^{B}\log (p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})$ , thus (D.19) holds with probability at least  $1 - \frac{C}{p_1p_2p_3}$ . By Lemma E.9,  $\log (\mathcal{M}_k(\mathcal{Y}') + 1 / 2) - \mathbb{E}\log (\mathcal{M}_k(\mathcal{Y}') + 1 / 2)$  has independent sub-Gaussian entries with  $\psi_{2}$  norm bounded by  $K_{0}\frac{e^{B}}{I}$ , then by Vershynin (2010), we have

$$
\left\| \mathcal {M} _ {k} \left(\mathcal {Z} _ {1}\right) \right\| \leq K _ {0} \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {B}}{I}} \left(\sqrt {p _ {- k}} + \sqrt {p _ {k}} + t\right)
$$

hold with probability at least  $1 - e^{-t^2}$ . By setting  $t = \sqrt{p_k}$ , we have (D.20) hold with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\underline{p}}$ . Next, for (D.21), note that

$$
\sup_{\| \mathbf{V}_{k}\| \leq 1,k = 1,2,3}\bigg\|\mathcal{Z}_{1}\times_{1}  \mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}  \mathbf{V}_{2}^{\top}\times_{3}  \mathbf{V}_{3}^{\top}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{F}} = \sup_{\substack{\| \mathbf{V}_{k}\| \leq 1,k = 1,2,3\\ \mathcal{S}\in \mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\| \mathcal{S}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}}\langle \mathcal{Z}_{1},[[\mathcal{S};\mathbf{V}_{1},\mathbf{V}_{2},\mathbf{V}_{3}]]\rangle .
$$

Since each entry of  $\mathcal{Z}_1$  has independent sub-Gaussian entry with  $\psi_{2}$  norm bounded by  $C_0\frac{e^B}{I}$ , by Lemma E.5, we have:

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\sqrt {\frac {e ^ {B}}{I}} \sup  _ {\| \mathbf {V} _ {k} \| \leq 1, k = 1, 2, 3} \left\| \mathcal {Z} _ {1} \times_ {1} \mathbf {V} _ {1} ^ {\top} \times_ {2} \mathbf {V} _ {2} ^ {\top} \times_ {3} \mathbf {V} _ {3} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} > C _ {1} \sqrt {d f}\right) \leq 2 \exp (- c \cdot d f),
$$

which gives (D.21). Finally, by Lemma E.10, we know that (D.22) holds with probability at least  $1 - c / (p_1p_2p_3)$ . Thus, applying union bounds on the above probabilistic events, we know that (D.19)-(D.22) hold with probability at least  $1 - c / (p_1p_2p_3)$  and the proof is finished.

D.4. Proof of Theorem 4.5. The proof is very similar to that of Theorem 4.3. Let  $g(\mathcal{P};\mathcal{N})$  be a mapping from  $\mathbb{R}^{p_1\times p_2\times p_3}$  to  $\mathbb{R}^{p_1\times p_2\times p_3}$ , such that

$$
[ g (\mathcal {P}; \mathcal {N}) ] _ {j k l} = \log \left(\frac {\mathcal {P} _ {j k l} + 1 / (2 \mathcal {N} _ {j k l})}{1 - \mathcal {P} _ {j k l} + 1 / (2 \mathcal {N} _ {j k l})}\right),
$$

then we have  $\tilde{\mathcal{X}}_{jkl}^{(0)} = g(\hat{\mathcal{P}},\mathcal{N})$  with  $\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{jkl} = \mathcal{Y}_{jkl} / \mathcal{N}_{jkl}$  for any  $j,k,l$ . Now we define

$$
\mathcal {P} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} = \hat {\mathcal {P}} _ {j k l} 1 _ {\left\{| \hat {\mathcal {P}} _ {j k l} - \mathcal {P} _ {j k l} | \leq \mathcal {P} _ {j k l} / 2 \right\}} + \mathcal {P} _ {j k l} 1 _ {\left\{| \hat {\mathcal {P}} _ {j k l} - \mathcal {P} _ {j k l} | > \mathcal {P} _ {j k l} / 2 \right\}},
$$

where  $\mathcal{P}_{jkl} \coloneqq s(\mathcal{X}_{jkl}^*)$ , and further denote  $\mathcal{Z} = \mathcal{Z}_1 + \mathcal{Z}_2$  with

$$
\mathcal {Z} _ {1} = g (\mathcal {P} ^ {\prime}; \mathcal {N}) - \mathbb {E} g (\mathcal {P} ^ {\prime}; \mathcal {N}), \quad \mathcal {Z} _ {2} = \mathbb {E} g (\mathcal {P} ^ {\prime}; \mathcal {N}) - \mathcal {X} ^ {*}.
$$

Again, we first impose the following conditions for the deterministic analysis, and then verify they actually hold with high probability.

$(A_{1})$

$$
\mathcal {P} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} = \hat {\mathcal {P}} _ {j k l}, \quad \forall (j, k, l) \in [ p _ {1} ] \times [ p _ {2} ] \times [ p _ {3} ] \tag {D.24}
$$

$(A_{2})$

$$
\| \mathcal {M} _ {k} \left(\mathcal {Z} _ {1}\right) \| \leq 2 K _ {0} \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {2 B}}{N}} \left(\sqrt {p _ {- k}} + \sqrt {p _ {k}}\right), \quad k = 1, 2, 3 \tag {D.25}
$$

$(A_{3})$

$$
\sup  _ {\left\| \mathbf {V} _ {k} \right\| \leq 1, k = 1, 2, 3} \left\| \mathcal {Z} _ {1} \times_ {1} \mathbf {V} _ {1} ^ {\top} \times_ {2} \mathbf {V} _ {2} ^ {\top} \times_ {3} \mathbf {V} _ {3} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq C _ {1} \frac {\sqrt {d f}}{\sqrt {N / e ^ {2 B}}}, \tag {D.26}
$$

$(A_4)$

$$
\sup  _ {\substack {\mathcal {T} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p _ {1} \times p _ {2} \times p _ {3}}, \\ \operatorname {rank} (\mathcal {T}) \leq (r _ {1}, r _ {2}, r _ {3}), \\ \| \mathcal {T} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq 1}} \left\langle - \hat {\mathcal {P}} + s (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}), \mathcal {T} \right\rangle \leq C _ {2} \frac {\sqrt {d f}}{\sqrt {N}}. \tag{D.27}
$$

By Lemma E.11, we know that given  $N > C_0Be^{3B}$  for some universal constant  $C_0$ ,

$$
\left| \mathbb {E} \left[ g \left(\mathcal {P} ^ {\prime}; \mathcal {N}\right) \right] _ {j k l} - \mathcal {X} _ {j k l} ^ {*} \right| \leq C \frac {e ^ {2 B}}{N},
$$

$$
\left\| g \left(\mathcal {P} ^ {\prime}; \mathcal {N}\right) - \mathbb {E} g \left(\mathcal {P} ^ {\prime}; \mathcal {N}\right) \right\| _ {\psi_ {2}} \leq C \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {2 B}}{N}}.
$$

Then one can combine this with conditions (D.24), (D.25) and (D.26) to obtain the initialization error by the same proof technique we used in Theorem 4.3:

$$
\left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(0)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq C \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {2 B}}{N}} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left(\sqrt {p _ {k} r _ {k}} + \sqrt {p _ {- k} r _ {k}}\right),
$$

as long as  $N \geq C\overline{p}e^{3B}$ .

Now we start to check the conditions in Theorem 3.1. Notice that the loss function  $L(\mathcal{X}) = -\sum_{jkl} \left( \mathcal{P}_{jkl} \mathcal{X}_{jkl} + \log \left( 1 - s(\mathcal{X}_{jkl}) \right) \right)$  is of  $\frac{1}{e^B + 3}$ -smoothness and  $\frac{1}{4}$ -strong convexity, then by Lemma E.1, we can set

$$
\alpha = \frac {1}{2 (e ^ {B} + 3)} <   \frac {\frac {1}{4 (e ^ {B} + 3)}}{\frac {1}{e ^ {B} + 3} + 1 / 4}, \quad \beta = \frac {1}{2} \leq \frac {1}{\frac {1}{e ^ {B} + 3} + 1 / 4}
$$

and  $L(\mathcal{X})$  satisfies  $\operatorname{RCG}(\alpha, \beta, \mathcal{C})$  with  $\mathcal{C}$  defined according to (3.3) and (4.6). In addition, we can also evaluate the scale of  $\xi$ :

$$
\xi := \sup_{\substack{\mathcal{T}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}},\\ \operatorname {rank}(\mathcal{T})\leq (r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}),\\ \| \mathcal{T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}}\left\langle -\hat{\mathcal{P}} +s(\mathcal{X}^{*}),\mathcal{T}\right\rangle \overset {\text{(D.27)}}{\leq}C_{2}\frac{\sqrt{df}}{\sqrt{N}}.
$$

Then as we have  $N\underline{\lambda}^2 \geq C\kappa^2 e^{3B}\sum_{k = 1}^{3}(p_kr_k + p_{-k}r_k)$ , it follows that

$$
\left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(0)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq C \frac {e ^ {2 B}}{N} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} (p _ {k} r _ {k} + p _ {- k} r _ {k}) \leq \frac {c \alpha \beta \underline {{\lambda}} ^ {2}}{\kappa^ {2}}.
$$

Thus the initialization condition is meet. In the meantime, one can also check that the signal-noise-ratio condition  $\underline{\lambda}^2 \geq C \frac{\kappa^4}{\alpha^3 \beta} \xi^2$  is also satisfied when  $\kappa$  and  $B$  are treated as constants. Thus by applying Theorem 3.1, we obtain

$$
\left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {T} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq \frac {\kappa^ {2}}{\alpha^ {2}} \xi^ {2} = C \kappa^ {2} e ^ {2 B} \frac {d f}{N}.
$$

Now we are going to show that conditions (D.24)-(D.27) hold with high probability. First of all,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {P} = \mathcal {P} ^ {\prime}\right) = 1 - \mathbb {P} \left(\exists (j, k, l), \mathcal {P} _ {j k l} \neq \mathcal {P} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime}\right) \\ \geq 1 - \sum_ {j, k, l} \left(\mathbb {P} \left(\hat {\mathcal {P}} _ {j k l} \leq \frac {\mathcal {P} _ {j k l}}{2}\right) + \mathbb {P} \left(\hat {\mathcal {P}} _ {j k l} \geq \frac {3}{4} \mathcal {P} _ {j k l}\right)\right) \\ \geq 1 - 2 \sum_ {j, k, l} \exp \left(- \frac {1}{2} \mathcal {N} _ {j k l} \mathcal {P} _ {j k l} ^ {2}\right) \\ \geq 1 - 2 p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3} \exp \left(- c N e ^ {- 2 B}\right) \\ \geq 1 - \frac {1}{p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last but one inequality comes from the assumption that  $\| \mathcal{X}^*\|_{\infty} < B$  and  $\mathcal{P} = s(\mathcal{X}^*)$ , and the last inequality comes from the condition that  $N \geq Ce^{2B}\log (\overline{p})$ , so (D.24) holds with probability at least  $1 - \frac{1}{p_1p_2p_3}$ . We can also prove that (D.25), (D.26), and (D.27) hold with probability at least  $1 - 1 / p_1p_2p_3$  as we did for the proof of (D.20), (D.21) and (D.22) in Theorem 4.3, and we omitted them here.

D.5. Proof of Theorem 4.4. Since we assume  $\max_k r_k \leq \min_k \sqrt{p_k}$ , it suffices to show the following inequalities:

$$
\inf  _ {\hat {\mathcal {X}}} \sup  _ {\mathcal {X} \in \mathcal {F} _ {p, r}} \mathbb {E} \left\| \hat {\mathcal {X}} - \mathcal {X} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \geq c I ^ {- 1} p _ {k} r _ {k}, \quad \forall k = 1, 2, 3
$$

We only need to prove (D.28) for  $k = 1$ . First of all, we let  $\mathbf{V} \in \{-1,1\}^{\lfloor \frac{p_k}{2}\rfloor \times r_k}$  such that each entry are i.i.d. Rademacher random variables (i.e.,  $\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{V}_{ij} = 1) = \mathbb{P}(\mathbf{V}_{ij} = -1) = 1 / 2, \forall i,j$ ). Then by Vershynin (2010),

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\sqrt {\lfloor \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rfloor} - \sqrt {r _ {1}} - t \leq \sigma_ {r _ {1}} (\mathbf {V}) \leq \| \mathbf {V} \| \leq \sqrt {\lfloor \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rfloor} + \sqrt {r _ {1}} + t\right) > 1 - e ^ {- \frac {t ^ {2}}{2}}.
$$

Taking  $t = c_0 \sqrt{r_1}$  for some sufficiently small constant  $c_0$ , since the above probability is positive, we know that there exists a  $\mathbf{V}_0 \in \{-1, 1\}^{\lfloor \frac{p_1}{2} \rfloor \times r_1}$ , such that

$$
\| \mathbf {V} _ {0} \| \leq \sqrt {\left\lfloor \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \right\rfloor} + (1 + c _ {0}) \sqrt {r _ {1}} \leq \sqrt {p _ {1} r _ {1}},
$$

and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {r _ {1}} (\mathbf {V} _ {0}) \geq \sqrt {\lfloor \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rfloor} - (1 + c _ {0}) \sqrt {r _ {1}} \geq \sqrt {\frac {p _ {1} - 1}{2}} - (1 + c _ {0}) C _ {2} ^ {- 1 / 2} r _ {1} \\ \geq \sqrt {\frac {p _ {1} - 1}{2}} - (1 + c _ {0}) C _ {1} C _ {2} ^ {- 1 / 2} p _ {1} ^ {1 / 2} \\ \geq \sqrt {\frac {p _ {1} - 1}{2}} - c _ {0} p _ {1} ^ {1 / 2} \geq \sqrt {\frac {p _ {1}}{3}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Here we obtain the last but one inequality by taking  $C_2 > C_1^2 / c_0^2$ .

In addition, for  $i = 1,2,\ldots ,N$ , let  $\mathbf{U}^{(i)}\in \{-1,1\}^{\lceil \frac{p_1}{2}\rceil \times r_1}$  be the i.i.d. copy of  $\mathbf{V}_0$ , and we denote

$$
\Omega^ {(i j)} = \left\{1 \leq a \leq \lceil \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rceil , 1 \leq b \leq r _ {1}: \mathbf {U} _ {a b} ^ {(i)} \neq \mathbf {U} _ {a b} ^ {(j)} \right\}.
$$

Since  $\left|\Omega^{(ij)}\right| \sim \mathrm{Binomial}\left(\lceil \frac{p_1}{2} \rceil r_1, \frac{1}{2}\right)$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\frac {\lceil \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rceil r _ {1}}{4} \leq \left| \Omega^ {(i j)} \right| \leq \frac {3 \lceil \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rceil r _ {1}}{4}, \forall i \neq j\right) \\ = 1 - \mathbb {P} \left(\exists i \neq j, \left| \Omega^ {(i j)} \right| <   \frac {\lceil \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rceil r _ {1}}{4} \mathrm {o r} \left| \Omega^ {(i j)} \right| > \frac {3 \lceil \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rceil r _ {1}}{4}, \forall i \neq j\right) \\ \geq 1 - N (N - 1) / 2 \left(\mathbb {P} \left(\left| \Omega^ {(i j)} \right| <   \frac {\lceil \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rceil r _ {1}}{4}\right) + \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \Omega^ {(i j)} \right| > \frac {3 \lceil \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rceil r _ {1}}{4}\right)\right) \tag {D.29} \\ = 1 - \frac {N (N - 1)}{2} \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \frac {\left| \Omega^ {(i j)} \right|}{\lceil \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rceil r _ {1}} - \frac {1}{2} \right| > \frac {1}{4}\right) \\ \geq 1 - N (N - 1) e ^ {- \lceil \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rceil r _ {1} / 3 2} \\ \geq 1 - N (N - 1) e ^ {- p _ {1} r _ {1} / 6 4}. \\ \end{array}
$$

In the meantime, by Vershynin (2010), we also have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\left\| \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {(i)} \right\| \leq \sqrt {\lceil \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rceil} + \sqrt {r _ {1}} + t, \forall 1 \leq i \leq N\right) \\ = 1 - \mathbb {P} \left(\exists i \in [ N ], \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {(i)} \right\| > \sqrt {\lceil \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \rceil} + \sqrt {r _ {1}} + t\right) \\ \geq 1 - N e ^ {- \frac {t ^ {2}}{2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Taking  $t = \sqrt{p_1r_1}$ , we see that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left\| \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {(i)} \right\| \leq 2 \sqrt {p _ {1} r _ {1}}, \forall 1 \leq i \leq N\right) \geq 1 - N e ^ {- p _ {1} r _ {1} / 2}. \tag {D.30}
$$

Combining (D.29) and (D.30), we know that with probability at least  $1 - N(N - 1)e^{-p_1r_1 / 64} - Ne^{-p_1r_1 / 2}$ ,

$$
\left. \left\lceil \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \right\rceil r _ {1} \leq \left\| \mathbf {U} ^ {(i)} - \mathbf {U} ^ {(j)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq 3 \left\lceil \frac {p _ {1}}{2} \right\rceil r _ {1}, \quad 1 \leq i <   j \leq N, \right. \tag {D.31}
$$

$$
\left\| \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {(i)} \right\| \leq 2 \sqrt {p _ {1} r _ {1}}, \quad 1 \leq i \leq N. \tag {D.32}
$$

By taking  $N = e^{cp_1r_1}$  for some small constant  $c$ , the probability defined in (D.30) is positive and thus there exists  $\left\{\mathbf{U}_1^{(i)},\ldots ,\mathbf{U}_1^{(N)}\right\} \subset \{-1,1\}^{\lceil \frac{p_1}{2}\rceil \times r_1}$  such that (D.31) and (D.32) hold.

Now we let  $S \in \mathbb{R}^{r_1 \times r_2 \times r_3}$  be a fixed core tensor such that

$$
\frac {B}{C \sqrt {\mu_ {1} \mu_ {2} \mu_ {3}}} \leq \min  _ {k} \sigma_ {r _ {k}} (\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {S})) \leq \max  _ {k} \| \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {S}) \| \leq \frac {B}{3 \sqrt {\mu_ {1} \mu_ {2} \mu_ {3}}} \tag {D.33}
$$

for some  $C > 3$ , and let  $\mathbf{U}_k \in \mathbb{O}_{p_k, r_k}$  be the orthogonal matrix such that  $\| \mathbf{U}_k \|_{2,\infty}^2 \leq \frac{\mu_k r_k}{p_k}$  for  $k = 2, 3$ . Let  $\mathcal{X}^{(i)} = \delta \cdot [[S; \tilde{\mathbf{U}}_1^{(i)}, \mathbf{U}_2, \mathbf{U}_3]]$  for some  $0 < \delta < 1$  with

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(i)} = \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {(i)} \\ \mathbf {V} _ {0} \end{array} \right].
$$

Let  $\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_1^{(i)} = \hat{\mathbf{U}}_1^{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}$  be the QR-decomposition of  $\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_1^{(i)}$  where  $\hat{\mathbf{U}}_1^{(i)}\in \mathbb{O}_{p_k,r_k}$  is an orthogonal matrix. Thus we can rewrite  $\mathcal{X}^{(i)} = [[\delta S\times_{1}\mathbf{R}^{(i)};\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)},\mathbf{U}_{2},\mathbf{U}_{3}]]$ . Note that by construction,  $\sigma_{r_1}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_1^{(i)}\right)\geq \sigma_{r_1}\left(\mathbf{V}_0\right)\geq \sqrt{\frac{p_1}{3}}$ , and we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \hat {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {2, \infty} ^ {2} = \max  _ {i} \left\| e _ {i} ^ {\top} \hat {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} = \max  _ {i} \left\| e _ {i} ^ {\top} \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(i)} \left(\mathbf {R} ^ {(i)}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq \max  _ {i} \frac {\left\| e _ {i} ^ {\top} \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{\sigma_ {r _ {1}} ^ {2} (\mathbf {R} ^ {(i)})} \leq \frac {\mu_ {k} r _ {k}}{p _ {k}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Here the last inequality comes from the facts that  $\left\| e_i^\top \tilde{\mathbf{U}}_1^{(i)}\right\|_2^2 = r_k$  for each  $i$  and  $\sigma_{r_1}\left(\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\right) = \sigma_{r_1}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_1^{(i)}\right)$ .

In the meantime, we also have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max _ {k} \left\| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\delta \mathcal {S} \times_ {1} \mathbf {R} ^ {(i)}) \right\| \leq \max _ {k} \| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {S}) \| \cdot \left\| \mathbf {R} ^ {(i)} \right\| \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(D . 3 3)}} {\leq} \frac {B}{3 \sqrt {\mu_ {1} \mu_ {2} \mu_ {3}}} \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(i)} \right\| \leq \frac {B}{3 \sqrt {\mu_ {1} \mu_ {2} \mu_ {3}}} \left(\left\| \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {(i)} \right\| + \| \mathbf {V} _ {0} \|\right) \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(D . 3 2)}} {\leq} \frac {B}{\sqrt {\mu_ {1} \mu_ {2} \mu_ {3}}} \sqrt {p _ {1} r _ {1}} \leq B \sqrt {\frac {\Pi_ {k = 1} ^ {3} p _ {k}}{\Pi_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mu_ {k} r _ {k}}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality comes from the assumption that  $\max_k r_k \leq \min_k \sqrt{p_k}$ . Thus we have  $\mathcal{X}^{(i)} \in \mathcal{F}_{\boldsymbol{p},\boldsymbol{r}}$  for each  $i \in [N]$ .

Now we provide the KL-divergence between  $\mathcal{Y}^{(i_1)}\sim \mathrm{Poisson}(I\exp (\mathcal{X}^{(i_1)}))$  and  $\mathcal{Y}^{(i_2)}\sim$  Poisson  $(I\exp (\mathcal{X}^{(i_2)}))$  for  $i_1\neq i_2$ . For any two Poisson distribution  $P\sim \mathrm{Poisson}(\lambda_1)$  and  $Q\sim \mathrm{Poisson}(\lambda_2)$ , the KL-divergence between  $P$  and  $Q$  is

$$
D _ {K L} (P | | Q) = \lambda_ {1} \log \left(\frac {\lambda_ {1}}{\lambda_ {2}}\right) + \lambda_ {2} - \lambda_ {1}.
$$

Thus we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} D _ {K L} \left(\mathcal {Y} ^ {(i _ {1})} | | \mathcal {Y} ^ {(i _ {2})}\right) = \sum_ {j, k, l} D _ {K L} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {(i _ {1})} | | \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {(i _ {2})}\right) \\ = I \sum_ {j, k, l} \left(\exp \left(\mathcal {X} _ {j k l} ^ {(i _ {1})}\right) \left(\mathcal {X} _ {j k l} ^ {(i _ {1})} - \mathcal {X} _ {j k l} ^ {(i _ {2})}\right) + \exp \left(\mathcal {X} _ {j k l} ^ {(i _ {2})}\right) - \exp \left(\mathcal {X} _ {j k l} ^ {(i _ {1})}\right)\right) \\ \stackrel {(a)} {=} I \sum_ {j, k, l} \frac {1}{2} \exp \left(\xi_ {j k l} ^ {(i _ {1}, i _ {2})}\right) \left(\mathcal {X} _ {j k l} ^ {(i _ {1})} - \mathcal {X} _ {j k l} ^ {(i _ {2})}\right) ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(b)} {\leq} \frac {1}{2} I e ^ {B} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(i)} - \mathcal {X} ^ {(j)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{2} I e ^ {B} \delta^ {2} \left\| [   [ S; \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {1})} - \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2} ^ {(i _ {2})}, \mathbf {U} _ {2}, \mathbf {U} _ {3} ]   ] \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {1}{2} I e ^ {B} \delta^ {2} \| \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {S}) \| ^ {2} \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {1})} - \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {2})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(\mathrm {D . 3 3})} {\leq} I \frac {\delta^ {2} B ^ {2} e ^ {B}}{1 8 \mu_ {1} \mu_ {2} \mu_ {3}} \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {1})} - \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {2})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \stackrel {(\mathrm {D . 3 1})} {\leq} I \frac {\delta^ {2} B ^ {2} e ^ {B}}{1 2 \mu_ {1} \mu_ {2} \mu_ {3}} p _ {1} r _ {1}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Here (a) is obtained by applying second order Taylor's expansion on exponential function, and  $\xi_{jkl}^{(i_1,i_2)}$  is some real number between  $\mathcal{X}_{jkl}^{(i_1)}$  and  $\mathcal{X}_{jkl}^{(i_2)}$ ; (b) comes from the fact that  $\mathcal{X}_{jkl}^{(i_1)} \vee \mathcal{X}_{jkl}^{(i_2)} \leq B$  by construction.

Besides,  $\forall i_1 \neq i_2$ , we also have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathcal {X} ^ {(i _ {1})} - \mathcal {X} ^ {(i _ {2})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} = \delta^ {2} \left\| [ S; \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {1})} - \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2} ^ {(i _ {2})}, \mathbf {U} _ {2}, \mathbf {U} _ {3} ] \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ \geq \delta^ {2} \sigma_ {r _ {1}} (\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {S})) ^ {2} \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {1})} - \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2} ^ {(i _ {2})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\stackrel {(D. 3 5)} {\geq} \frac {\delta^ {2} B ^ {2}}{C ^ {2} \mu_ {1} \mu_ {2} \mu_ {3}} \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {1} ^ {\left(i _ {1}\right)} - \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {2} ^ {\left(i _ {2}\right)} \right\| _ {F} ^ {2}
$$

$$
\stackrel {\text {(D . 3 1)}} {\geq} \frac {\delta^ {2} B ^ {2}}{4 C ^ {2} \mu_ {1} \mu_ {2} \mu_ {3}} p _ {1} r _ {1}.
$$

Then by generalized Fano Lemma (Yang and Barron, 1999),

$$
\inf _ {\hat {\mathcal {X}}} \sup _ {\mathcal {X} \in \{\mathcal {X} ^ {(i)} \} _ {i = 1} ^ {N}} \mathbb {E} \left\| \hat {\mathcal {X}} - \mathcal {X} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \geq c \frac {\delta^ {2} B ^ {2}}{\mu_ {1} \mu_ {2} \mu_ {3}} p _ {1} r _ {1} \left(1 - \frac {I B ^ {2} e ^ {B} \delta^ {2} p _ {1} r _ {1} / (1 2 \mu_ {1} \mu_ {2} \mu_ {3}) + \log 2}{c p _ {1} r _ {1} - 1}\right).
$$

By setting  $\delta^2 = \frac{c\mu_1\mu_2\mu_3}{100IB^2e^B}$ , we obtain (D.29).

![](images/debb88c30c2e4e68217a158b73281939667efae688fc5633e747bdb402e3970e.jpg)

D.6. Proof of Theorem 4.6. The proof of Theorem 4.6 is similar to that of Theorem 4.4. We construct the same  $\{\mathcal{X}^{(1)},\ldots \mathcal{X}^{(N)}\}_{i = 1}^{N}$  as we did in the proof of Theorem 4.4. Notice that for any two binomial distributions  $P = \operatorname {Binomial}(n,p)$ ,  $Q = \operatorname {Binomial}(n,q)$ , the KL-divergence between  $P$  and  $Q$  is

$$
D _ {K L} (P | | Q) = \log \left(\frac {p}{q}\right) n p + \log \left(\frac {1 - p}{1 - q}\right) n (1 - p).
$$

Now let  $P_{1} = \operatorname{Binomial}(n, s(x_{1}))$  and  $P_{2} = \operatorname{Binomial}(n, s(x_{2}))$ , for some  $-B \leq x_{1} \leq x_{2} \leq B$  where  $s(x) = 1 / (1 + e^{-x})$ , then the KL-divergence of  $P_{1}$  and  $P_{2}$  is

$$
\begin{array}{l} D _ {K L} (P _ {1} | | P _ {2}) = n \left(\log \left(\frac {1 + e ^ {- x _ {2}}}{1 + e ^ {- x _ {1}}}\right) \frac {1}{1 + e ^ {- x _ {1}}} + \log \left(\frac {1 + e ^ {- x _ {2}}}{1 + e ^ {- x _ {1}}} e ^ {x _ {2} - x _ {1}}\right) \frac {e ^ {- x _ {1}}}{1 + e ^ {- x _ {1}}}\right) \\ = n \left(\log \left(\frac {1 + e ^ {- x _ {2}}}{1 + e ^ {- x _ {1}}}\right) + \frac {e ^ {- x _ {1}}}{1 + e ^ {- x _ {1}}} (x _ {2} - x _ {1})\right) \\ = n \left(\log \left(1 + e ^ {- x _ {2}}\right) - \log \left(1 + e ^ {- x _ {1}}\right) + \frac {e ^ {- x _ {1}}}{1 + e ^ {- x _ {1}}} \left(x _ {2} - x _ {1}\right)\right) \\ \stackrel {(a)} {=} \frac {n e ^ {- \xi}}{(1 + e ^ {- \xi}) ^ {2}} = \frac {n}{2 + e ^ {\xi} + e ^ {- \xi}} \leq \frac {n}{4}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Here  $(a)$  comes from the second order Taylor's expansion of  $f(x) = \log (1 + e^{-x})$ . Then we obtain

$$
D _ {K L} \left(\mathcal {Y} ^ {(i _ {1})} | | \mathcal {Y} ^ {(i _ {2})}\right) \leq c \delta^ {2} \max  _ {j k l} \mathcal {N} _ {j k l} p _ {1} r _ {1} \leq c \delta^ {2} \min  _ {j k l} \mathcal {N} _ {j k l} p _ {1} r _ {1},
$$

given  $\mathcal{Y}_{jkl}^{(i)}\sim \mathrm{Binomial}(\mathcal{N}_{jkl},s(\mathcal{X}_{jkl}))$  independently. By the similar argument in the proof of Theorem 4.4, we can show

$$
\inf  _ {\hat {\mathcal {X}}} \sup  _ {\mathcal {X} \in \mathcal {F} _ {p, r}} \left\| \hat {\mathcal {X}} - \mathcal {X} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \geq c N ^ {- 1} \left(r _ {1} r _ {2} r _ {3} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} p _ {k} r _ {k}\right),
$$

and the proof is finished.

D.7. Proof of Proposition 5.1. Assume  $\sigma = 1$  without loss of generality. We only prove for  $k = 1$ , while the proof for other modes follows in the same way. We prove the two scenarios separately.

(a) Sub-Gaussian tensor PCA. Let  $\mathbf{U}_1 \in \mathbb{O}_{p_1, r_1}$  be the left singular subspace of  $\mathcal{M}_1(\mathcal{X}^*)$  and  $\mathbf{U}_{1\perp} \in \mathbb{O}_{p_1, p_1 - r_1}$  be the orthogonal subspace of  $\mathbf{U}_1$ . Let  $\mathcal{Z} := \tilde{\mathcal{X}} - \mathcal{X}^*$  and define the event:

$$
A = \left\{\frac {9}{1 0} \sqrt {p _ {- 1}} \leq \sigma_ {p _ {1}} (\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {Z})) \leq \| \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {Z}) \| \leq \frac {1 1}{1 0} \sqrt {p _ {- 1}} \right\}.
$$

Since  $\mathcal{M}_1(\mathcal{Z})$  has independent mean-zero unit-variance sub-Gaussian random variables, by the concentration of singular values of random matrix (Vershynin, 2010, Corollary 5.35), we have

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\sqrt {p _ {- 1}} - \sqrt {p _ {1}} - t \leq \sigma_ {p _ {1}} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {Z})\right) \leq \| \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {Z}) \| \leq \sqrt {p _ {- 1}} + \sqrt {p _ {1}} + t\right) \geq 1 - C e ^ {- c t ^ {2}}.
$$

Since  $p_1 \leq cp_{-1}$ , taking  $t = c\sqrt{p_1}$  in the above inequality yields that  $\mathbb{P}(A) \geq 1 - 2e^{-cp_{-1}}$ . Now we provide a lower bound for  $\sigma_{p_1}\left(\mathcal{M}_1(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right)$  assuming  $A$  holds. By definition,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_{p_{1}}^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right) = \inf_{\substack{u\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}\\ \| u\| = 1}}\left\| u^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right\|_{2}^{2} \\ = \inf_{\substack{u\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}\\ \| u\| = 1}}\left(\left\| u^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{1}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right\|_{2}^{2} + \left\| u^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{1\perp}\mathbf{U}_{1\perp}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Since

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| u ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {1} \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {\top} \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\tilde {\mathcal {X}}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \geq \| u ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {1} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \sigma_ {r _ {1}} ^ {2} \left(\mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {\top} \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\tilde {\mathcal {X}})\right) \\ \geq \| u ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {1} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \left(\sigma_ {r _ {1}} \left(\mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {\top} \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*})\right) - \| \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {Z}) \|\right) ^ {2} \\ \geq \| u ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {1} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \cdot (\underline {{\lambda}} - 1. 1 \sqrt {p _ {- 1}}) ^ {2} \geq \frac {1}{2} \underline {{\lambda}} ^ {2} \| u ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {1} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| u ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {1 \perp} \mathbf {U} _ {1 \perp} ^ {\top} \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\tilde {\mathcal {X}}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} = \left\| u ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {1 \perp} \mathbf {U} _ {1 \perp} ^ {\top} \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {Z}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \geq \| u ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {1 \bot} \mathbf {U} _ {1 \bot} ^ {\top} \| \cdot \sigma_ {p _ {1}} ^ {2} (\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {Z})) \geq \frac {4 p _ {- 1}}{5} \| u ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {1 \bot} \| _ {2} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

we further have

$$
\sigma_{p_{1}}^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right)\geq \inf_{\substack{u\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}\\ \| u\| = 1}}\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}\| u^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\| +\frac{4p_{-1}}{5}\| u^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{1\perp}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\geq \frac{4p_{-1}}{5}. \tag{D.36}
$$

Recall  $\delta_{1}$  is the median of non-zero singular values of  $\mathcal{M}_1(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})$ , we then have

$$
\delta_ {1} \geq \sigma_ {p _ {1}} (\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\tilde {\mathcal {X}})) \geq \frac {9 \sqrt {p _ {- 1}}}{1 0}.
$$

On the other hand, since  $r_1 = o(p_1)$

$$
\delta_ {1} \leq \sigma_ {r _ {1} + 1} (\mathcal {M} _ {k} (\tilde {\mathcal {X}})) \leq \| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {Z}) \| \leq \frac {1 1}{1 0} \sqrt {p _ {- 1}}.
$$

Thus,

$$
\sigma_ {r _ {1} + 1} (\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\tilde {\mathcal {X}})) \leq 1. 5 \delta_ {1},
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {r _ {1}} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\tilde {\mathcal {X}})\right) \geq \underline {{\lambda}} - \frac {1 1 \sqrt {p - 1}}{1 0} \tag {D.37} \\ \geq 2 \sqrt {p _ {- 1}} \geq 1. 5 \delta_ {1}. \\ \end{array}
$$

(D.37) implies  $\hat{r}_1 = r_1$  and the proof is finished.

(b) Tensor regression. Following the proof of Theorem 4.2, we assume each entry of  $\mathcal{A}_i$  comes from i.i.d.  $N(0,1/n)$  and  $\varepsilon \sim N(0,\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{I}_n)$ . Then we have  $\tilde{\mathcal{X}} = \sum_{i=1}^{n}y_i\mathcal{A}_i = \mathcal{A}^*(y)$ . Define  $\mathcal{Z} \coloneqq \tilde{\mathcal{X}} - \mathcal{A}^*\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{X}^*) = \mathcal{A}^*(\varepsilon)$  and define the linear operator  $\mathcal{A}_1: \mathbb{R}^{p_1 \times p_{-1}} \to \mathbb{R}^n$ :

$$
\left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} (\mathbf {X})\right) _ {i} = \langle \mathcal {M} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {i}\right), \mathbf {X} \rangle .
$$

Let  $\mathcal{A}_1^*$  be the adjoint operator of  $\mathcal{A}_1$ . We consider the following events:

$$
\begin{array}{l} A _ {1} := \left\{\frac {9}{1 0} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}} \leq \sigma_ {p _ {1}} (\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {Z})) \leq \| \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {Z}) \| \leq \frac {1 1}{1 0} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}} \right\}, \\ A _ {2} := \left\{\left(1 - \frac {1}{2 \kappa \sqrt {r _ {1}}}\right) \leq \frac {\| \mathcal {A} _ {1} (\mathbf {X}) \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{\| \mathbf {X} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}} \leq \left(1 + \frac {1}{2 \kappa \sqrt {r _ {1}}}\right) \text {f o r a n y r a n k - 2 r _ {1} m a t r i x} \mathbf {X} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p _ {1} \times p _ {- 1}} \right\}, \\ A _ {3} := \left\{\sigma_ {r _ {1} + 1} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathcal {A} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right)\right)\right) \leq \frac {1 1}{1 0} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}} \| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \right\}, \\ A _ {4} := \left\{\sigma_ {p _ {1} - r _ {1}} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathcal {A} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right)\right)\right) \geq \frac {9}{1 0} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}} \| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \right\}. \\ \end{array}
$$

We first show that each of the above events hold with probability at least  $1 - Ce^{-cp_{-1}}$ .

-  $A_{1}$ : Note that conditional on  $\varepsilon$ ,  $\mathcal{M}_1(\mathcal{Z}) = \mathcal{A}_1^* (\varepsilon)$  has i.i.d. mean-zero normal entries with variance  $\| \varepsilon \| _2^2$ . Therefore, by random matrix theory, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\sigma_ {p _ {1}} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} (\varepsilon)\right) \geq \frac {\| \varepsilon \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\sqrt {p _ {- 1}} - \sqrt {p _ {1}} - t\right) | \varepsilon\right) \geq 1 - 2 e ^ {- t ^ {2} / 2}, \tag {D.38} \\ \mathbb {P} \left(\| \mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} (\varepsilon)) \| \leq \frac {\| \varepsilon \| _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}} \left(\sqrt {p _ {- 1}} + \sqrt {p _ {1}} + t\right) | \varepsilon\right) \geq 1 - 2 e ^ {- t ^ {2} / 2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

In addition, by the concentration of Chi-square random variable (Birgé, 2001, Lemma 8.1), we have

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\| \varepsilon \| _ {2} ^ {2} \geq 1 + 2 \sqrt {x / n} + 2 x / n\right) \leq e ^ {- x},
$$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\| \varepsilon \| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq 1 - 2 \sqrt {x / n}\right) \leq e ^ {- x}.
$$

Taking  $x = cn$ , we have with probability at least  $1 - 2\exp (-cp_{-1})$  that  $\| \varepsilon \|_2 \geq 9/10$ . Now we specify  $t = c\sqrt{p_{-1}}$  in (D.38) and obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\sigma_ {p _ {1}} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} (\varepsilon)\right) \geq \frac {4}{5} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}}\right) \\ \geq \mathbb {P} \left(\| \varepsilon \| \geq \frac {4}{5}\right) \cdot \mathbb {P} \left(\sigma_ {p _ {1}} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} (\varepsilon)\right) \geq \frac {4}{5} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}} \mid \| \varepsilon \| \geq 1 / 2\right) \end{array} \tag {D.39} \\ \geq \left(1 - e ^ {- c p _ {- 1}}\right) \mathbb {P} \left(\sigma_ {p _ {1}} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} (\varepsilon)\right) \geq \| \varepsilon \| \cdot \frac {8}{9} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}} \mid \| \varepsilon \| \geq 1 / 2\right) \\ \geq \left(1 - e ^ {- c p _ {- 1}}\right) \left(1 - 2 e ^ {- c p _ {- 1}}\right) \geq 1 - C e ^ {- c p _ {- 1}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Similarly one can prove  $\mathbb{P}\left(\| \mathcal{A}_1^* (\varepsilon)\| \leq \frac{6}{5}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\right) \geq 1 - Ce^{-cp_{-1}}$ . Thus, we have proved  $\mathbb{P}(A_1) \geq 1 - Ce^{-cp_{-1}}$ .

-  $A_{2}$ : By (Recht, Fazel and Parrilo, 2010, Lemma 4.3), when  $n \geq C\kappa p_{-1}r_1^{3/2}\log r_1$ , one has  $\mathbb{P}(A_2) \geq 1 - \exp(-cp_{-1}r_1)$ .

-  $A_{3}, A_{4}$ : Since  $\mathcal{M}_1(\mathcal{A}_i)$  has i.i.d. Gaussian entries and  $\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{M}_1(\mathcal{X}^*)) = r_1$ , we can assume that the last  $p_1 - r_1$  rows of  $\mathcal{M}_1(\mathcal{X}^*)$  are zeros without loss of generality. Therefore, we can write

$$
\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) = \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {X} _ {1} \\ \mathbf {O} \end{array} \right], \qquad \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {A} _ {i}) = \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {A} _ {i 1} \\ \mathbf {A} _ {i 2} \end{array} \right]
$$

for  $\mathbf{X}_1, \mathbf{A}_{i1} \in \mathbb{R}^{r_1 \times p_1}$ ,  $\mathbf{A}_{i2} \in \mathbb{R}^{p_1 - r_1 \times p_{-1}}$ . Then we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {r _ {1} + 1} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathcal {A} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right)\right)\right) = \sigma_ {r _ {1} + 1} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \left\langle \mathbf {A} _ {i 1}, \mathbf {X} _ {1} \right\rangle \left[ \begin{array}{l} \mathbf {A} _ {i 1} \\ \mathbf {A} _ {i 2} \end{array} \right]\right) \tag {D.40} \\ = \inf _ {\mathbf {U} \in \mathbb {O} _ {p _ {1}, p _ {1} - r _ {1}}} \sup _ {u \in \mathbf {U}} \left\| u ^ {\top} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \langle \mathbf {A} _ {i 1}, \mathbf {X} _ {1} \rangle \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {A} _ {i 1} \\ \mathbf {A} _ {i 2} \end{array} \right] \right\| _ {2} \leq \left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \langle \mathbf {A} _ {i 1}, \mathbf {X} _ {1} \rangle \mathbf {A} _ {i 2} \right\| \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\sigma_ {p _ {1} - r _ {1}} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathcal {A} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*})\right)\right) = \sup  _ {\mathbf {U} \in \mathbb {O} _ {p _ {1}, r _ {1}}} \inf  _ {u \in \mathbf {U}} \left\| u ^ {\top} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \langle \mathbf {A} _ {i 1}, \mathbf {X} _ {1} \rangle \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {A} _ {i 1} \\ \mathbf {A} _ {i 2} \end{array} \right] \right\| _ {2}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \geq \sup  _ {\mathbf {U} \in \mathbb {O} _ {p _ {1} - r _ {1}, r _ {1}}} \inf  _ {u \in \mathbf {U}} \left\| u ^ {\top} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \langle \mathbf {A} _ {i 1}, \mathbf {X} _ {1} \rangle \mathbf {A} _ {i 2} \right\| _ {2} \\ = \sigma_ {p _ {1} - r _ {1}} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \langle \mathbf {A} _ {i 1}, \mathbf {X} _ {1} \rangle \mathbf {A} _ {i 2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Recall that  $\mathbf{A}_{i1}$  and  $\mathbf{A}_{i2}$  are independent. Conditional on  $\mathbf{A}_{i1}$ ,  $\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\mathbf{A}_{i1},\mathbf{X}_1\rangle\mathbf{A}_{i2}$  is a  $(p_1 - r_1)$ -by- $p_{-1}$  random matrix with i.i.d.  $N\left(0,\tilde{\sigma}^2\right)$  entries where  $\tilde{\sigma}^2 \coloneqq \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\mathbf{A}_{i1},\mathbf{X}_1\rangle^2$ . Therefore, by random matrix theory,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \langle \mathbf {A} _ {i 1}, \mathbf {X} _ {1} \rangle \mathbf {A} _ {i 2} \right\| \leq \tilde {\sigma} (\sqrt {p _ {- 1}} - \sqrt {p _ {1} - r _ {1}} + t) \Bigg | \{\mathbf {A} _ {i 1} \} _ {i = 1} ^ {n}\right) \leq 1 - 2 e ^ {- t ^ {2} / 2},
$$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\sigma_ {p _ {1} - r _ {1}} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \langle \mathbf {A} _ {i 1}, \mathbf {X} _ {1} \rangle \mathbf {A} _ {i 2}\right) \geq \tilde {\sigma} (\sqrt {p _ {- 1}} - \sqrt {p _ {1} - r _ {1}} - t) \Bigg | \{\mathbf {A} _ {i 1} \} _ {i = 1} ^ {n}\right) \leq 1 - 2 e ^ {- t ^ {2} / 2}.
$$

On the other hand, since  $n^2\tilde{\sigma}^2 /\| \mathbf{X}_1\|_{\mathrm{F}}^2\sim \chi_n^2$  , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\tilde {\sigma} ^ {2} \leq \frac {\| \mathbf {X} _ {1} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}}{n} \left(1 + 2 \sqrt {x / n} + 2 x / n\right)\right) \geq 1 - e ^ {- x}, \\ \mathbb {P} \left(\tilde {\sigma} ^ {2} \geq \frac {\| \mathbf {X} _ {1} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}}{n} \left(1 - 2 \sqrt {x / n}\right)\right) \geq 1 - e ^ {- x}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Combining the concentrations inequalities above and following the same argument as (D.39), we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\sigma_ {p _ {1} - r _ {1}} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \langle \mathbf {A} _ {i 1}, \mathbf {X} _ {1} \rangle \mathbf {A} _ {i 2}\right) \geq \frac {9}{1 0} \| \mathbf {X} _ {1} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}}\right) \leq 1 - C e ^ {- c p _ {- 1}}, \tag {D.42} \\ \mathbb {P} \left(\left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \langle \mathbf {A} _ {i 1}, \mathbf {X} _ {1} \rangle \mathbf {A} _ {i 2} \right\| \leq \frac {1 1}{1 0} \| \mathbf {X} _ {1} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}}\right) \leq 1 - C e ^ {- c p _ {- 1}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

(D.40), (D.41) and (D.42) together imply that  $\mathbb{P}(A_3) \geq 1 - Ce^{-cp_{-1}}$ ,  $\mathbb{P}(A_4) \geq 1 - Ce^{-cp_{-1}}$ .

By union bound, we know that  $\mathbb{P}(A_1 \cap A_2 \cap A_3 \cap A_4) \geq 1 - Ce^{-cp_{-1}}$ . Now we show that  $\hat{r}_1 = r_1$  under the event  $A_1 \cap A_2 \cap A_3 \cap A_4$ . On the one hand,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {r _ {1} + 1} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\tilde {\mathcal {X}})\right) = \sigma_ {r _ {1} + 1} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathcal {A} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right)\right) + \mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} (\varepsilon))\right) \\ \leq \sigma_ {r _ {1} + 1} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathcal {A} _ {1} (\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}))\right) + \| \mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} (\varepsilon) \| \\ \stackrel {A _ {1}, A _ {3}} {\leq} \frac {1 1}{1 0} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}} (\| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} + 1) \leq \frac {6}{5} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}} \| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Here in the last inequality we use the assumption that  $1 = \sigma \leq c\| \mathcal{X}^{*}\|_{\mathrm{F}}$ . Meanwhile,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {p _ {1} - r _ {1}} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\tilde {\mathcal {X}})\right) \geq \sigma_ {p _ {1} - r _ {1}} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathcal {A} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right)\right)\right) - \| \mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} (\varepsilon) \| \\ \geq \frac {9}{1 0} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}} \left(\| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} - 1\right) \geq \frac {4}{5} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}} \| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Consequently, the median  $\delta_{1}$  satisfies

$$
1. 5 \delta_ {1} \geq 1. 5 \sigma_ {p _ {1} - r _ {1}} (\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\tilde {\mathcal {X}})) \geq \frac {6}{5} \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}} \| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \geq \sigma_ {r _ {1} + 1} (\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\tilde {\mathcal {X}})). \tag {D.43}
$$

On the other hand,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {r _ {1}} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathcal {A} _ {1} (\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}))\right) = \inf _ {\mathbf {U} \in \mathbb {O} _ {p _ {1}, r _ {1} - 1}} \left\| (\mathbf {I} _ {p _ {1}} - \mathbf {U U} ^ {\top}) \mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathcal {A} _ {1} (\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*})) \right\| \\ = \inf_{\mathbf{U}\in \mathbb{O}_{p_{1},r_{1} - 1}}\sup_{\substack{u\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{1}},\| u\| = 1\\ v\in \mathbb{R}^{p - 1},\| v\| = 1}}\Big\langle (\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}} - \mathbf{UU}^{\top})\mathcal{A}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{X}^{*})),uv^{\top}\Big\rangle  \\ = \inf_{\mathbf{U}\in \mathbb{O}_{p_{1},r_{1} - 1}}\sup_{\substack{u\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{1}},\| u\| = 1\\ v\in \mathbb{R}^{p - 1},\| v\| = 1}}\Big\langle \mathcal{A}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{X}^{*})),\mathcal{A}_{1}((\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}} - \mathbf{UU}^{\top})uv^{\top})\Big\rangle  \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
