\begin{array}{l} \stackrel{\text{Lemma E.7}}{\geq}\inf_{\mathbf{U}\in \mathbb{O}_{p_{1},r_{1} - 1}}\sup_{\substack{u\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{1}},\| u\| = 1\\ v\in \mathbb{R}^{p - 1},\| v\| = 1}}\Big\langle \mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{X}^{*}),(\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}} - \mathbf{UU}^{\top})uv^{\top}\Big\rangle  - \frac{1}{2\kappa\sqrt{r_{1}}}\| \mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{X}^{*})\|_{\mathrm{F}} \\ = \inf  _ {\mathbf {U} \in \mathbb {O} _ {p _ {1}, r _ {1} - 1}} \left\| \left(\mathbf {I} _ {p _ {1}} - \mathbf {U} \mathbf {U} ^ {\top}\right) \mathcal {M} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right) \right\| - \frac {1}{2 \kappa \sqrt {r _ {1}}} \| \mathcal {M} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ = \sigma_ {r _ {1}} \| \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \| - \frac {1}{2 \kappa \sqrt {r _ {1}}} \| \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \geq \lambda_ {1} - \frac {1}{2 \kappa \sqrt {r _ {1}}} \kappa \sqrt {\bar {r}} \lambda_ {1} \geq \frac {\lambda_ {1}}{2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then,

$$
\sigma_ {r _ {1}} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\tilde {\mathcal {X}})\right) \geq \sigma_ {r _ {1}} \left(\mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathcal {A} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {1} \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right)\right)\right) - \left\| \mathcal {A} _ {1} ^ {*} (\varepsilon) \right\|
$$

$$
\geq \frac {\lambda_ {1}}{2} - \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}} \| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}}
$$

(D.44)

$$
\begin{array}{l} \geq \left(\frac {1}{2 \kappa \sqrt {r _ {1}}} - \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}}\right) \| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \geq 2 \sqrt {\frac {p _ {- 1}}{n}} \| \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \geq 1. 5 \delta_ {1}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Here the last but two inequalities comes from the assumption on  $n$ . Now  $\hat{r}_1 = r_1$  is implied by (D.43) and (D.44) and the proof is finished.

# APPENDIX E: TECHNICAL LEMMAS

We collect the technical lemmas with their proofs in this section. They are widely used in the proof of the major theorems.

E.1. Proof of Lemma 2.1. Let  $\mathcal{X} \coloneqq [[S; \mathbf{U}_1, \mathbf{U}_2, \mathbf{U}_3]]$ , we first calculate the partial gradient of  $L(S, \mathbf{U}_1, \mathbf{U}_2, \mathbf{U}_3)$  with respect to  $\mathbf{U}_1$  by chain rule:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathbf {U} _ {1 , i j}} = \sum_ {a = 1} ^ {p _ {1}} \sum_ {b = 1} ^ {p _ {2}} \sum_ {c = 1} ^ {p _ {3}} \frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathcal {X} _ {a b c}} \cdot \frac {\partial \mathcal {X} _ {a b c}}{\partial \mathbf {U} _ {1 , i j}} \\ = \sum_ {a = 1} ^ {p _ {1}} \sum_ {b = 1} ^ {p _ {2}} \sum_ {c = 1} ^ {p _ {3}} \frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathcal {X} _ {a b c}} \cdot \left(I _ {\{a = i \}} \sum_ {l _ {2} = 1} ^ {r _ {2}} \sum_ {l _ {3} = 1} ^ {r _ {3}} \mathcal {S} _ {j l _ {2} l _ {3}} \mathbf {U} _ {2, b l _ {2}} \mathbf {U} _ {3, c l _ {3}}\right) \\ = \sum_ {b = 1} ^ {p _ {2}} \sum_ {c = 1} ^ {p _ {3}} \frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathcal {X} _ {i b c}} \left(\sum_ {l _ {2} = 1} ^ {r _ {2}} \sum_ {l _ {3} = 1} ^ {r _ {3}} \mathcal {S} _ {j l _ {2} l _ {3}} \mathbf {U} _ {2, b l _ {2}} \mathbf {U} _ {3, c l _ {3}}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second identity comes from the following fact:

$$
\mathcal {X} _ {a b c} = \sum_ {l _ {1} = 1} ^ {r _ {1}} \sum_ {l _ {2} = 1} ^ {r _ {2}} \sum_ {l _ {3} = 1} ^ {r _ {3}} \mathcal {S} _ {l _ {1} l _ {2} l _ {3}} \mathbf {U} _ {1, a l _ {1}} \mathbf {U} _ {2, b l _ {2}} \mathbf {U} _ {3, c l _ {3}}
$$

In the meantime, one can verify that

$$
\left(\mathcal {M} _ {1} (\nabla L (\mathcal {X})) \left(\mathbf {U} _ {3} \otimes \mathbf {U} _ {2}\right) \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {S}) ^ {\top}\right) _ {i j} = \sum_ {k _ {1} = 1} ^ {p _ {2}} \sum_ {k _ {2} = 1} ^ {p _ {3}} \sum_ {k _ {3} = 1} ^ {r _ {2}} \sum_ {k _ {4} = 1} ^ {r _ {3}} \frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathcal {X} _ {i k _ {1} k _ {2}}} \cdot \mathbf {U} _ {2, k _ {1} k _ {3}} \mathbf {U} _ {3, k _ {2} k _ {4}} S _ {j k _ {3} k _ {4}},
$$

which is exactly what we calculated for  $\frac{\partial L}{\partial\mathbf{U}_{1,ij}}$  (by changing the order of summation). The partial gradient for  $\mathbf{U}_2$  and  $\mathbf{U}_3$  can be similarly calculated. For core tensor  $S$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathcal {S} _ {i j k}} = \sum_ {a = 1} ^ {p _ {1}} \sum_ {b = 1} ^ {p _ {2}} \sum_ {c = 1} ^ {p _ {3}} \frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathcal {X} _ {a b c}} \cdot \frac {\partial \mathcal {X} _ {a b c}}{\partial \mathcal {S} _ {i j k}} \\ = \sum_ {a = 1} ^ {p _ {1}} \sum_ {b = 1} ^ {p _ {2}} \sum_ {c = 1} ^ {p _ {3}} \frac {\partial L}{\partial \mathcal {X} _ {a b c}} \cdot \mathbf {U} _ {1, a i} \mathbf {U} _ {2, b j} \mathbf {U} _ {3, c k} \\ = \left(\llbracket \nabla L (\mathcal {X}); \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {\top}, \mathbf {U} _ {2} ^ {\top}, \mathbf {U} _ {3} ^ {\top} \rrbracket\right) _ {i j k}, \\ \end{array}
$$

which has finished the proof of this lemma.

![](images/c57d0a731060058a274d94068eec3522c69523a60f3c8b81cfccbc325565dccb.jpg)

# E.2. Proof of Proposition 2.1. Let

$$
\left(\hat {\mathcal {S}}, \hat {\mathbf {U}} _ {1}, \hat {\mathbf {U}} _ {2}, \hat {\mathbf {U}} _ {3}\right) = \underset {\mathcal {S}, \mathbf {U} _ {1}, \mathbf {U} _ {2}, \mathbf {U} _ {3}} {\arg \min} L (\llbracket \mathcal {S}; \mathbf {U} _ {1}, \mathbf {U} _ {2}, \mathbf {U} _ {3} \rrbracket ; D) + \frac {a}{2} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} _ {r _ {k}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}.
$$

We claim  $\hat{\mathbf{U}}_k^\top \hat{\mathbf{U}}_k = b^2\mathbf{I}_{r_k}$  for  $k = 1,2,3$ . Otherwise, consider the QR-decomposition  $\hat{\mathbf{U}}_k = \tilde{\mathbf{U}}_k\mathbf{R}_k$  where  $\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_k \in \mathbb{O}_{p_k,r_k}$  and define  $\bar{S} = b^{-3}[[\hat{S};\mathbf{R}_1,\mathbf{R}_2,\mathbf{R}_3]]$ ,  $\bar{\mathbf{U}}_k = b\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_k$ . Then clearly  $F(\hat{S},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_1,\hat{\mathbf{U}}_2,\hat{\mathbf{U}}_3) > F(\hat{S},\bar{\mathbf{U}}_1,\bar{\mathbf{U}}_2,\bar{\mathbf{U}}_3)$  and makes contradiction.

Similarly, For arbitrary rank- $(r_1, r_2, r_3)$  tensor  $\mathcal{X} = [[S; \mathbf{U}_1, \mathbf{U}_2, \mathbf{U}_3]]$ , we can redecompose it as  $\mathcal{X} = [[S^*; \mathbf{U}_1^*, \mathbf{U}_2^*, \mathbf{U}_3^*]]$  such that  $\mathbf{U}_k^{*^\top} \mathbf{U}_k^* = b^2 \mathbf{I}_{r_k}$ . Then it follows that

$$
L (\hat {\mathcal {X}}; D) = F (\bar {\mathcal {S}}, \bar {\mathbf {U}} _ {1}, \bar {\mathbf {U}} _ {2}, \bar {\mathbf {U}} _ {3}) \leq F (\mathcal {S} ^ {*}, \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {*}, \mathbf {U} _ {2} ^ {*}, \mathbf {U} _ {3} ^ {*}) = L (\mathcal {X}; D).
$$

Thus,  $\hat{\mathcal{X}} = [[\hat{S};\hat{\mathbf{U}}_1,\hat{\mathbf{U}}_2,\hat{\mathbf{U}}_3]]$  is a rank-constraint minimizer of  $L(\mathcal{X};D)$ .

E.3. Lemmas for Main Theory. We collect the technical lemmas that are used to develop Theorem 3.1 in this section.

The first lemma builds the relationship between the standard strongly convex and smooth assumption with RCG condition 3.1.

Lemma E.1 Consider a continuously differentiable function  $f$ , given a convex domain  $\mathbb{G}$ , suppose for any  $x, y \in \mathbb{G}$ , we have

$$
\frac {m}{2} \left\| x - y \right\| ^ {2} \leq f (y) - f (x) - \langle \nabla f (x), y - x \rangle \leq \frac {M}{2} \left\| x - y \right\| ^ {2},
$$

then

$$
\langle \nabla f (x) - \nabla f (y), x - y \rangle \geq \frac {m M}{m + M} \left\| x - y \right\| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{m + M} \left\| \nabla f (x) - \nabla f (y) \right\| ^ {2}.
$$

Proof of Lemma E.1. See Nesterov (1998).

The next lemma establishes the relationship between  $\left\| \mathcal{X}^{(t)} - \mathcal{X}^{*}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}$  and  $E^{(t)}$  in the proof of Theorem 3.1.

Lemma E.2 Suppose  $\mathcal{X}^* = [[\mathcal{S}^*; \mathbf{U}_1^*, \mathbf{U}_2^*, \mathbf{U}_3^*]], \quad \mathbf{U}_k^{\top} \mathbf{U}_k^* = b^2 \mathbf{I}_{r_k}, k = 1, 2, 3,$ $\overline{\lambda} = \max_k \| \mathcal{M}_k(\mathcal{X}^*) \|, \text{ and } \underline{\lambda} = \min_k \sigma_{r_k}(\mathcal{M}_k(\mathcal{X}^ {*})).$  Let  $\mathcal{X} = [[\mathcal{S}; \mathbf{U}_1, \mathbf{U}_2, \mathbf{U}_3]]$  be another Tucker low-rank tensor with  $\mathbf{U}_k \in \mathbb{R}^{p_k \times r_k}$ ,  $\| \mathbf{U}_k \| \leq (1 + c_d)b$ , and  $\max_k \| \mathcal{M}_k(\mathcal{S}) \| \leq (1 + c_d)\frac{\overline{\lambda}}{b^3}$  for some constant  $c_d > 0$ . Define

$$
E := \min _ {\mathbf {R} _ {k} \in \mathbb {O} _ {p _ {k}, r _ {k}}, k = 1, 2, 3} \left(\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \| \mathbf {U} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + \left\| \mathcal {S} - \llbracket \mathcal {S} ^ {*}; \mathbf {R} _ {1} ^ {\top}, \mathbf {R} _ {2} ^ {\top}, \mathbf {R} _ {3} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}\right).
$$

Then we have

$$
E \leq b ^ {- 6} \left(7 + \frac {1 2 b ^ {8}}{\underline {{\lambda}} ^ {2}} C _ {d}\right) \| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + 2 b ^ {- 2} C _ {d} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2},
$$

$$
\| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq 4 b ^ {6} \left(1 + (3 + 2 c _ {d}) ^ {2} (1 + c _ {d}) ^ {4} \overline {{\lambda}} ^ {2} b ^ {- 8}\right) E,
$$

where  $C_d \coloneqq 1 + 7\overline{\lambda}^2 b^{-8}\left((1 + c_d)^6 + (1 + c_d)^4 (2 + c_d)^2\right)$ .

Proof of Lemma E.2. First, note that

$$
\| \llbracket S; \mathbf {R} _ {1}, \mathbf {R} _ {2}, \mathbf {R} _ {3} \rrbracket - \mathcal {S} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} = b ^ {- 3} \| \llbracket S; \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {1}, \mathbf {U} _ {2} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {2}, \mathbf {U} _ {3} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {3} \rrbracket - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}}.
$$

Then we can decompose  $\left[\mathcal{S};\mathbf{U}_1^*\mathbf{R}_1,\mathbf{U}_2^*\mathbf{R}_2,\mathbf{U}_3^*\mathbf{R}_3\right] - \mathcal{X}^*$  into seven terms,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \llbracket \mathcal {S}; \mathbf {U} _ {1} + \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {1} - \mathbf {U} _ {1}, \mathbf {U} _ {2} + \mathbf {U} _ {2} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {2} - \mathbf {U} _ {2}, \mathbf {U} _ {3} + \mathbf {U} _ {3} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {3} - \mathbf {U} _ {3} \rrbracket - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \\ = \left(\mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right) + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {S} \times_ {k} \left(\mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k}\right) \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \\ + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {2} \mathcal {S} \times_ {k} \left(\mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k}\right) \times_ {k + 1} \left(\mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 1} - \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1}\right) \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \\ + \mathcal {S} \times_ {1} \left(\mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {1} - \mathbf {U} _ {1}\right) \times_ {2} \mathbf {U} _ {2} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {2} \times_ {3} \left(\mathbf {U} _ {3} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {3} - \mathbf {U} _ {3}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

By the Inequality of arithmetic and geometric means, we further have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathcal {S} - [   [ \mathcal {S} ^ {*}; \mathbf {R} _ {1} ^ {\top}, \mathbf {R} _ {2} ^ {\top}, \mathbf {R} _ {3} ^ {\top} ]   ] \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} = \| [   [ \mathcal {S}; \mathbf {R} _ {1}, \mathbf {R} _ {2}, \mathbf {R} _ {3} ]   ] - \mathcal {S} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ \leq 7 b ^ {- 6} \| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + 7 b ^ {- 6} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left(\| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {S}) \| ^ {2} \| \mathbf {U} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \| \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \otimes \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2}\right) \\ + 7 b ^ {- 6} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {2} \left(\| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {S}) \| ^ {2} \| \mathbf {U} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \| \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} - \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} \| \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \| ^ {2}\right) \\ + 7 b ^ {- 6} \left\| \mathcal {M} _ {2} (\mathcal {S}) \right\| ^ {2} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {1} - \mathbf {U} _ {1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {3} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {3} - \mathbf {U} _ {3} \right\| ^ {2} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {2} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {2} \right\| ^ {2} \\ \leq 7 b ^ {- 6} \| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + 7 b ^ {- 6} \left((1 + c _ {d}) ^ {6} \overline {{\lambda}} ^ {2} b ^ {- 2} + (1 + c _ {d}) ^ {4} (2 + c _ {d}) ^ {2} \overline {{\lambda}} ^ {2} b ^ {- 2}\right) \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \| \mathbf {U} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}. \\ = 7 b ^ {- 6} \| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + 7 \overline {{\lambda}} ^ {2} b ^ {- 8} \left((1 + c _ {d}) ^ {6} + (1 + c _ {d}) ^ {4} (2 + c _ {d}) ^ {2}\right) \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \| \mathbf {U} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Since the above inequality holds for any orthogonal matrices  $\mathbf{R}_1, \mathbf{R}_2, \mathbf{R}_3$ , it follows that (E.1)

$$
\begin{array}{l} E = \min _ {\mathbf {R} _ {k} \in \mathbb {O} _ {p _ {k} \times r _ {k}}, k = 1, 2, 3} \left(\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \| \mathbf {U} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + \left\| \mathcal {S} - \mathcal {S} ^ {*} \times_ {1} \mathbf {R} _ {1} ^ {\top} \times_ {2} \mathbf {R} _ {2} ^ {\top} \times_ {3} \mathbf {R} _ {3} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}\right) \\ \leq 7 b ^ {- 6} \left\| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + C _ {d} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \min _ {\mathbf {R} _ {k} \in \mathbb {O} _ {r _ {k}}} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $C_d = 1 + 7\overline{\lambda}^2 b^{-8}\left((1 + c_d)^6 + (1 + c_d)^4 (2 + c_d)^2\right)$ .

Now let  $\mathbf{U}_k = \tilde{\mathbf{U}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}^\top$  be the SVD of  $\mathbf{U}_k$ , where  $\tilde{\mathbf{U}} \in \mathbb{O}_{p_k,r_k}$ ,  $\tilde{\mathbf{V}} \in \mathbb{O}_{r_k}$ , and  $\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}} = \mathrm{diag}(\sigma_1,\ldots,\sigma_{r_k})$  is a diagonal matrix. Then we have

$$
\min  _ {\mathbf {R} _ {k} \in \mathbb {O} _ {r _ {k}}} \| \mathbf {U} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} = \min  _ {\mathbf {R} _ {k} \in \mathbb {O} _ {r _ {k}}} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} - b \tilde {\mathbf {U}} \tilde {\mathbf {V}} ^ {\top} + b \tilde {\mathbf {U}} \tilde {\mathbf {V}} ^ {\top} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq 2 \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} \tilde {\mathbf {V}} ^ {\top} - b \tilde {\mathbf {U}} \tilde {\mathbf {V}} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + 2 \min  _ {\mathbf {R} _ {k} \in \mathbb {O} _ {r _ {k}}} \left\| b \tilde {\mathbf {U}} \tilde {\mathbf {V}} ^ {\top} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}. \tag {E.2} \\ = 2 \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} - b \mathbf {I} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + 2 \min  _ {\mathbf {R} _ {k} \in \mathbb {O} _ {r _ {k}}} \left\| b \tilde {\mathbf {U}} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

On the one hand,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} - b \mathbf {I} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} = b ^ {2} \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} / b - \mathbf {I} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ = b ^ {2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {r _ {k}} \left(\frac {\sigma_ {i}}{b} - 1\right) ^ {2} \leq b ^ {2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {r _ {k}} \left(\frac {\sigma_ {i} ^ {2}}{b ^ {2}} - 1\right) ^ {2} = b ^ {- 2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {r _ {k}} \left(\sigma_ {i} ^ {2} - b ^ {2}\right) ^ {2} \\ = b ^ {- 2} \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} ^ {2} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} = b ^ {- 2} \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {V}} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} ^ {2} \tilde {\mathbf {V}} ^ {\top} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ = b ^ {- 2} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {* \top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} = b ^ {- 2} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Here we use the inequality  $(x - 1)^2 \leq (x^2 - 1)^2$  for any non-negative number  $x$ . On the other hand, since  $\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_k$  and  $\mathbf{U}_k^* / b$  have orthonormal columns and span the left singular subspaces of  $\mathcal{M}_k(\mathcal{X})$  and  $\mathcal{M}_k(\mathcal{X}^*)$  respectively, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} = \| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {X}) - \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \geq \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k \perp} ^ {\top} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {X}) - \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*})\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ = \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k \perp} ^ {\top} \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} = \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k \perp} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} / b\right) \left(\mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} / b\right) ^ {\top} \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {X} ^ {*}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ \geq \sigma_ {r _ {k}} ^ {2} \left(\mathcal {M} _ {k} \left(\mathcal {X} ^ {*}\right)\right) \cdot \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k \perp} ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} / b \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \geq \underline {{\lambda}} ^ {2} \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k \perp} ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} / b \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}\in \mathbb{O}_{p_k - r_k,r_k}$  is the perpendicular orthogonal matrix of  $\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_k$ . Then it follows by (Cai and Zhang, 2018, Lemma 1) that

$$
\min  _ {\mathbf {R} _ {k} \in \mathbb {O} _ {r _ {k}}} \left\| b \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq 2 b ^ {2} \left\| \tilde {\mathbf {U}} _ {k \perp} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} / b\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq 2 b ^ {2} \frac {\left\| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}}{\underline {{\lambda}} ^ {2}}. \tag {E.4}
$$

Combing (E.2), (E.3) and (E.4), we have:

$$
\min _ {\mathbf {R} _ {k} \in \mathbb {O} _ {r _ {k}}} \| \mathbf {U} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq 2 b ^ {- 2} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + 4 b ^ {2} \frac {\| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}}{\underline {{\lambda}} ^ {2}}.
$$

Then by (E.1), we finally obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} E \leq 7 b ^ {- 6} \| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + C _ {d} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \min _ {\mathbf {R} _ {k} \in \mathbb {O} _ {r _ {k}}} \| \mathbf {U} _ {k} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \\ \leq b ^ {- 6} \left(7 + \frac {1 2 b ^ {8}}{\underline {{\lambda}} ^ {2}} C _ {d}\right) \| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + 2 b ^ {- 2} C _ {d} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {\top} \mathbf {U} _ {k} - b ^ {2} \mathbf {I} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Next, we prove the second inequality. To this end, we now denote

$$
(\mathbf{R}_{1},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}) = \operatorname *{arg  min}_{\substack{\mathbf{R}_{k}\in \mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}\\ k = 1,2,3}}\left\{\sum_{k = 1}^{3}\| \mathbf{U}_{k} - \mathbf{U}_{k}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{k}\|_{F}^{2} + \left\| \mathcal{S} - \llbracket \mathcal{S}^{*};\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\top},\mathbf{R}_{2}^{\top},\mathbf{R}_{3}^{\top}\rrbracket \right\|_{F}^{2}\right\} .
$$

Let  $\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{S}} = \mathcal{S}^{*} - [[\mathcal{S};\mathbf{R}_{1},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}]]$  and  $\mathbf{H}_k = \mathbf{U}_k^* -\mathbf{U}_k\mathbf{R}_k^\top$ . Then, we have

$$
\mathcal {X} ^ {*} = \left(\mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} + \llbracket \mathcal {S}; \mathbf {R} _ {1}, \mathbf {R} _ {2}, \mathbf {R} _ {3} \rrbracket\right) \times_ {1} \left(\mathbf {H} _ {1} + \mathbf {U} _ {1} \mathbf {R} _ {1} ^ {\top}\right) \times_ {2} \left(\mathbf {H} _ {2} + \mathbf {U} _ {2} \mathbf {R} _ {2} ^ {\top}\right) \times_ {3} \left(\mathbf {H} _ {3} + \mathbf {U} _ {3} \mathbf {R} _ {3} ^ {\top}\right)
$$

and it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq \| \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} \times_ {1} \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {*} \times_ {2} \mathbf {U} _ {2} ^ {*} \times_ {3} \mathbf {U} _ {3} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \| \mathcal {S} \times_ {k} \mathbf {H} _ {k} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {2} \| \mathcal {S} \times_ {k} \mathbf {H} _ {k} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 1} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 1} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} + \| \mathcal {S} \times_ {1} \mathbf {H} _ {1} \mathbf {R} _ {1} \times_ {2} \mathbf {U} _ {2} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {2} \times_ {3} \mathbf {H} _ {3} \mathbf {R} _ {3} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \leq b ^ {3} \left\| \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {S}) \right\| \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \otimes \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \right\| \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {k} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {2} \left\| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {S}) \right\| \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {k} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {k + 1} \right\| \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \right\| \\ + \left\| \mathcal {M} _ {3} (\mathcal {S}) \right\| \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {1} \right\| \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {2} ^ {*} \right\| \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {3} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \leq b ^ {3} \left\| \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} + (1 + c _ {d}) ^ {3} \bar {\lambda} b ^ {- 1} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {k} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} + (1 + c _ {d}) ^ {2} (2 + c _ {d}) \bar {\lambda} b ^ {- 1} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {k} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ = b ^ {3} \left\| \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} + (3 + 2 c _ {d}) (1 + c _ {d}) ^ {2} \bar {\lambda} b ^ {- 1} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {k} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Thus,

$$
\| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {X} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq 4 \left(b ^ {6} \| \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + (3 + 2 c _ {d}) ^ {2} (1 + c _ {d}) ^ {4} \bar {\lambda} ^ {2} b ^ {- 2} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \| \mathbf {H} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}\right).
$$

Suppose  $s, u_1, u_2, u_3$  are real values and  $\varepsilon_s, \varepsilon_{u_1}, \varepsilon_{u_2}, \varepsilon_{u_3}$  are small perturbations, then by simple calculation we can see that

(E.5)

$$
(s + \varepsilon_ {s}) (u _ {1} + \varepsilon_ {u _ {2}}) (u _ {1} + \varepsilon_ {u _ {2}}) (u _ {3} + \varepsilon_ {u _ {3}}) = s u _ {1} u _ {2} u _ {3} + \varepsilon_ {s} u _ {1} u _ {2} u _ {3} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} s \varepsilon_ {u _ {k}} u _ {k + 1} u _ {k + 2} + h _ {\varepsilon},
$$

where  $h_{\varepsilon}$  is a high-order perturbation term. The following lemma can be seen as a tensor version of (E.5), which plays a key role in the proof of Theorem 3.1.

Lemma E.3 Suppose  $\mathcal{X}^{*} = [[S^{*};\mathbf{U}_{1}^{*},\mathbf{U}_{2}^{*},\mathbf{U}_{3}^{*}]],\mathcal{X} = [[S;\mathbf{U}_{1},\mathbf{U}_{2},\mathbf{U}_{3}]]$  with  $S,S^{*}\in \mathbb{R}^{r_1\times r_2\times r_3}$ ,  $\mathbf{U}_k,\mathbf{U}_k^*\in \mathbb{R}^{p_k\times r_k},\mathbf{R}_k\in \mathbb{O}_{r_k},k = 1,2,3$ . Let

$$
\mathcal {X} _ {k} = \mathcal {S} \times_ {k} \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2}, \quad \mathbf {H} _ {k} = \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} - \mathbf {U} _ {k} \mathbf {R} _ {k} ^ {\top}, \quad k = 1, 2, 3
$$

$$
\mathcal {X} _ {\mathcal {S}} = [ [ \mathcal {S} ^ {*}; \mathbf {U} _ {1} \mathbf {R} _ {1} ^ {\top}, \mathbf {U} _ {2} \mathbf {R} _ {2} ^ {\top}, \mathbf {U} _ {3} \mathbf {R} _ {3} ^ {\top} ] ], \quad \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} = \mathcal {S} ^ {*} - [ [ \mathcal {S}; \mathbf {R} _ {1}, \mathbf {R} _ {2}, \mathbf {R} _ {3} ] ].
$$

Then we have the following decomposition of  $\mathcal{X}^*$ :

$$
\mathcal {X} ^ {*} = \mathcal {X} _ {S} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} (\mathcal {X} _ {k} - \mathcal {X}) + \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon}, \| \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq B _ {2} B _ {3} ^ {3 / 2} + 3 B _ {1} B _ {2} B _ {3} + 3 B _ {1} ^ {2} B _ {3}.
$$

Here,

$$
B _ {1} := \max  _ {k} \left\{\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} \|, \| \mathbf {U} _ {k} ^ {*} \| \right\}, \quad B _ {2} := \max  _ {k} \left\{\| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {S}) \|, \| \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {S} ^ {*}) \| \right\},
$$

$$
B _ {3} := \max  \left\{\| \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}, \| \mathbf {H} _ {1} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}, \| \mathbf {H} _ {2} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}, \| \mathbf {H} _ {3} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \right\}.
$$

Proof of Lemma E.3. Since  $S^{*} = [[S; \mathbf{R}_{1}, \mathbf{R}_{2}, \mathbf{R}_{3}]] + \mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{S}}$

$$
\mathcal {X} ^ {*} = \llbracket \mathcal {S}; \mathrm {U} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathrm {R} _ {1}, \mathrm {U} _ {2} ^ {*} \mathrm {R} _ {2}, \mathrm {U} _ {3} ^ {*} \mathrm {R} _ {3} \rrbracket + \llbracket \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}}; \mathrm {U} _ {1} ^ {*}, \mathrm {U} _ {2} ^ {*}, \mathrm {U} _ {3} ^ {*} \rrbracket . \tag {E.6}
$$

For the first term on the right hand side of (E.6), since  $\mathbf{U}_k^* = \mathbf{U}_k\mathbf{R}_k^\top + \mathbf{H}_k$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \llbracket \mathcal {S}; \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {1}, \mathbf {U} _ {2} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {2}, \mathbf {U} _ {3} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {3} \rrbracket = \llbracket \mathcal {S}; \mathbf {U} _ {1} + \mathbf {H} _ {1} \mathbf {R} _ {1}, \mathbf {U} _ {2} + \mathbf {H} _ {2} \mathbf {R} _ {2}, \mathbf {U} _ {3} + \mathbf {H} _ {3} \mathbf {R} _ {3} \rrbracket \\ = \mathcal {X} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {S} \times_ {k} \mathbf {U} _ {k} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 2} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 2} + \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)} \\ = \mathcal {X} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {S} \times_ {k} \mathbf {U} _ {k} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \times_ {k + 2} \left(\mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} ^ {*} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 2} - \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2}\right) + \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)} \\ = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {X} _ {k} - 2 \mathcal {X} + \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)} = \llbracket S; \mathbf {H} _ {1} \mathbf {R} _ {1}, \mathbf {H} _ {2} \mathbf {R} _ {2}, \mathbf {H} _ {3} \mathbf {R} _ {3} \rrbracket + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {S} \times_ {k} \mathbf {U} _ {k} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 1} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 1} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 2} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 2} \\ = \llbracket \mathcal {S} ^ {*} - \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}}; \mathbf {H} _ {1}, \mathbf {H} _ {2}, \mathbf {H} _ {3} \rrbracket + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} (\mathcal {S} ^ {*} - \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}}) \times_ {k} \mathbf {U} _ {k} \mathbf {R} _ {k} ^ {\top} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 1} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

For the second term on the right hand side of (E.6), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} [ \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}}; \mathbf {U} _ {1} ^ {*}, \mathbf {U} _ {2} ^ {*}, \mathbf {U} _ {3} ^ {*} ] \\ = \left(\mathcal {S} ^ {*} - \mathcal {S} \times_ {1} \mathbf {R} _ {1} \times_ {2} \mathbf {R} _ {2} \times_ {3} \mathbf {R} _ {3}\right) \times_ {1} \left(\mathbf {H} _ {1} + \mathbf {U} _ {1} \mathbf {R} _ {1} ^ {\top}\right) \times_ {2} \left(\mathbf {H} _ {2} + \mathbf {U} _ {2} \mathbf {R} _ {2} ^ {\top}\right) \times_ {3} \left(\mathbf {H} _ {3} + \mathbf {U} _ {3} \mathbf {R} _ {3} ^ {\top}\right) \\ = \mathcal {X} _ {\mathcal {S}} - \mathcal {X} + \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(2)}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(2)} = \llbracket \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}}; \mathbf {H} _ {1}, \mathbf {H} _ {2}, \mathbf {H} _ {3} \rrbracket + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} \times_ {k} \mathbf {U} _ {k} \mathbf {R} _ {k} ^ {\top} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 1} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 2} \\ + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} \times_ {k} \mathbf {H} _ {k} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 1} ^ {\top} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 2} ^ {\top}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then it follows that

$$
\mathcal {X} ^ {*} = \mathcal {X} _ {\mathcal {S}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {X} _ {k} - 3 \mathcal {X} + (\mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)} + \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(2)}).
$$

Since

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} = \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)} + \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(2)} = [   [ \mathcal {S} ^ {*}; \mathbf {H} _ {1}, \mathbf {H} _ {2}, \mathbf {H} _ {3} ]   ] + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {S} ^ {*} \times_ {k} \mathbf {U} _ {k} \mathbf {R} _ {k} ^ {\top} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 1} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 2} \\ + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} \times_ {k} \mathbf {H} _ {k} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 1} ^ {\top} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 2} ^ {\top}, \\ \end{array}
$$

we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathcal {H} _ {\varepsilon} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq \| [   [ \mathcal {S} ^ {*}; \mathbf {H} _ {1}, \mathbf {H} _ {2}, \mathbf {H} _ {3} ]   ] \| _ {\mathrm {F}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathcal {S} ^ {*} \times_ {k} \mathbf {U} _ {k} \mathbf {R} _ {k} ^ {\top} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 1} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {H} _ {k + 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}} \times_ {k} \mathbf {H} _ {k} \times_ {k + 1} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 1} ^ {\top} \times_ {k + 2} \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 2} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ = \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {1} \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {S} ^ {*}) \left(\mathbf {H} _ {3} \otimes \mathbf {H} _ {2}\right) ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} \mathbf {R} _ {k} ^ {\top} \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {S} ^ {*}) \left(\mathbf {H} _ {k + 2} \otimes \mathbf {H} _ {k + 1}\right) ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {H} _ {k} \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}}) \left(\mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 2} ^ {\top} \otimes \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 1} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ \leq \| \mathbf {H} _ {1} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \| \mathcal {M} _ {1} (\mathcal {S} ^ {*}) \| \| \mathbf {H} _ {3} \otimes \mathbf {H} _ {2} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k} \mathbf {R} _ {k} ^ {\top} \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {S} ^ {*}) \right\| \| \mathbf {H} _ {k + 2} \otimes \mathbf {H} _ {k + 1} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \\ + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} \| \mathbf {H} _ {k} \mathcal {M} _ {k} (\mathcal {H} _ {\mathcal {S}}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \left\| \mathbf {U} _ {k + 2} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 2} ^ {\top} \otimes \mathbf {U} _ {k + 1} \mathbf {R} _ {k + 1} ^ {\top} \right\| \\ \leq B _ {3} ^ {3 / 2} B _ {2} + 3 B _ {3} B _ {1} B _ {2} + 3 B _ {3} B _ {1} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, we have finished the proof.

E.4. Other Technical Lemmas. We collect additional technical lemmas in this section.

Lemma E.4 Suppose  $\mathbf{X},\mathbf{Z}\in \mathbb{R}^{m\times n}$  and  $\mathbf{Y} = \mathbf{X} + \mathbf{Z}$ ,  $\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{X}) = r$ . If the leading  $r$  left and right singular vectors of  $\mathbf{Y}$  are  $\hat{\mathbf{U}}\in \mathbb{O}_{m,r}$  and  $\hat{\mathbf{V}}\in \mathbb{O}_{n,r}$ , then

$$
\max \left\{\left\| \hat {\mathbf {U}} _ {\perp} ^ {\top} \mathbf {X} \right\|, \left\| \mathbf {X} \hat {\mathbf {V}} _ {\perp} \right\| \right\} \leq 2 \| \mathbf {Z} \|, \max \left\{\left\| \hat {\mathbf {U}} _ {\perp} ^ {\top} \mathbf {X} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}}, \left\| \mathbf {X} \hat {\mathbf {V}} _ {\perp} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \right\} \leq \min \{2 \sqrt {r} \| \mathbf {Z} \|, 2 \| \mathbf {Z} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \}.
$$

Proof of Lemma E.4. See (Zhang and Han, 2018, Lemma 6).

Lemma E.5 Assume all the entries of  $\mathcal{Z} \in \mathbb{R}^{p_1 \times p_2 \times p_3}$  are independent mean-zero random variables with bounded Orlicz- $\psi_2$  norm:

$$
\| \mathcal {Z} _ {i j k} \| _ {\psi_ {2}} = \sup  _ {q \geq 1} \mathbb {E} \left(| \mathcal {Z} _ {i j k} | ^ {q}\right) ^ {1 / q} / q ^ {1 / 2} \leq \sigma . \tag {E.7}
$$

Then there exist some universal constants  $C, c$ , such that

$$
\sup_{\substack{\mathcal{T}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}},\| \mathcal{T}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1,\\ \operatorname {rank}(\mathcal{T})\leq (r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})}}\langle \mathcal{Z},\mathcal{T}\rangle \leq C\sigma \left(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3} + \sum_{k = 1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}\right)^{1 / 2}
$$

with probability at least  $1 - \exp \left(-c\sum_{k = 1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}\right)$ .

Proof of Lemma E.5. First of all, By (Zhang and Xia, 2018, Lemma 7), for any  $k = 1, 2, 3$ , we can construct  $\varepsilon$ -net  $\left\{\mathbf{V}_k^{(1)}, \ldots, \mathbf{V}_k^{(N_k)}\right\}$  for  $\{\mathbf{V}_k \in \mathbb{R}^{p_k \times r_k} : \| \mathbf{V}_k \| \leq 1\}$  such that

$$
\sup  _ {\mathbf {V} _ {k}: \| \mathbf {V} _ {k} \| \leq 1} \min  _ {i \leq N _ {k}} \left\| \mathbf {V} _ {k} - \mathbf {V} _ {k} ^ {(i)} \right\| \leq \varepsilon
$$

with  $N_{k}\leq \left(\frac{4 + \varepsilon}{\varepsilon}\right)^{p_{k}r_{k}}$  . Also, we can construct  $\varepsilon$  -net  $\{S^{(1)},\ldots ,S^{(N_S)}\}$  for  $\{S\in \mathbb{R}^{r_1\times r_2\times r_3}:$ $\| S\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1\}$  such that

$$
\sup_{\mathcal{S}: \| \mathcal{S}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}\min_{i\leq N_{\mathcal{S}}}\left\| \mathcal{S} - \mathcal{S}^{(i)}\right\| \leq \varepsilon
$$

with  $N_{\mathcal{S}} \leq ((2 + \varepsilon) / \varepsilon)^{r_1 r_2 r_3}$ . We use  $\mathcal{I}$  to denote the index set  $[N_{\mathcal{S}}] \times [N_1] \times [N_2] \times [N_3]$ . Now suppose we have

$$
\left(\mathcal {S} ^ {*}, \mathbf {V} _ {1} ^ {*}, \mathbf {V} _ {2} ^ {*}, \mathbf {V} _ {3} ^ {*}\right) = \underset { \begin{array}{c} \mathcal {S} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {r _ {1} \times r _ {2} \times r _ {3}}, \| \mathcal {S} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq 1 \\ \mathbf {V} _ {l} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {p _ {l} \times r _ {l}}, \| \mathbf {V} _ {l} \| \leq 1 \end{array} } {\arg \max } \left\langle \mathcal {Z}, [   [ \mathcal {S}; \mathbf {V} _ {1}, \mathbf {V} _ {2}, \mathbf {V} _ {3} ]   ] \right\rangle , \tag {E.8}
$$

and denote

$$
T := \left\langle \mathcal {Z}, \llbracket S ^ {*}; \mathbf {V} _ {1} ^ {*}, \mathbf {V} _ {2} ^ {*}, \mathbf {V} _ {3} ^ {*} \rrbracket \right\rangle .
$$

Then we can find some index  $i = (i_0,i_1,i_2,i_3)\in \mathcal{I}$ , such that

$$
\left\| \mathcal {S} ^ {*} - \mathcal {S} ^ {(i _ {0})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq \varepsilon , \quad \left\| \mathbf {V} _ {k} ^ {*} - \mathbf {V} _ {k} ^ {(i _ {k})} \right\| \leq \varepsilon , k = 1, 2, 3
$$

and it follows that for any  $j,k,l$ , by taking  $\varepsilon = 1 / 14$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} T = \left\langle \mathcal {Z}, \llbracket S ^ {(i _ {0})}; \mathbf {V} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {1})}, \mathbf {V} _ {2} ^ {(i _ {2})}, \mathbf {V} _ {3} ^ {(i _ {3})} \rrbracket \right\rangle + T - \left\langle \mathcal {Z}, \llbracket S ^ {(i _ {0})}; \mathbf {V} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {1})}, \mathbf {V} _ {2} ^ {(i _ {2})}, \mathbf {V} _ {3} ^ {(i _ {3})} \rrbracket \right\rangle \\ = \left\langle \mathcal {Z}, \llbracket S ^ {(i _ {0})}; \mathbf {V} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {1})}, \mathbf {V} _ {2} ^ {(i _ {2})}, \mathbf {V} _ {3} ^ {(i _ {3})} \rrbracket \right\rangle + \left\langle \mathcal {Z}, \llbracket S ^ {*}; \mathbf {V} _ {1} ^ {*}, \mathbf {V} _ {2} ^ {*}, \mathbf {V} _ {3} ^ {*} \rrbracket - \llbracket S ^ {(i _ {0})}; \mathbf {V} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {1})}, \mathbf {V} _ {2} ^ {(i _ {2})}, \mathbf {V} _ {3} ^ {(i _ {3})} \rrbracket \right\rangle \\ \leq \left\langle \mathcal {Z}, \llbracket S ^ {(i _ {0})}; \mathbf {V} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {1})}, \mathbf {V} _ {2} ^ {(i _ {2})}, \mathbf {V} _ {3} ^ {(i _ {3})} \rrbracket \right\rangle + \left(3 \varepsilon + 3 \varepsilon^ {2} + \varepsilon^ {3}\right) T \\ \leq \left\langle \mathcal {Z}, \llbracket S ^ {(i _ {0})}; \mathbf {V} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {1})}, \mathbf {V} _ {2} ^ {(i _ {2})}, \mathbf {V} _ {3} ^ {(i _ {3})} \rrbracket \right\rangle + T / 2 \\ \end{array}
$$

and  $T\leq 2\left\langle \mathcal{Z},[[\mathcal{S}^{(i_0)};\mathbf{V}_1^{(i_1)},\mathbf{V}_2^{(i_2)},\mathbf{V}_3^{(i_3)}]]\right\rangle$

Notice that for any fixed  $S$ ,  $\{\mathbf{V}_k\}_{k=1}^3$  with  $\|S\|_F \leq 1$  and  $\|\mathbf{V}_k\| \leq 1$ , we have  $\|[S; \mathbf{V}_1, \mathbf{V}_2, \mathbf{V}_3]\|_F \leq 1$ . Since  $\mathcal{Z}_{jkl}$  are independent sub-Gaussian random variables with  $\| \mathcal{Z}_{jkl} \|_{\psi_2} \leq \sigma$ , by Hoeffding-type inequality for sub-Gaussian random variables' summation (Vershynin, 2010), we have

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left| \left\langle \mathcal {Z}, [   [ \mathcal {S} ^ {(i _ {0})}; \mathbf {V} _ {1} ^ {(i _ {1})}, \mathbf {V} _ {2} ^ {(i _ {2})}, \mathbf {V} _ {3} ^ {(i _ {3})} ]   ] \right\rangle \right| \geq t\right) \leq \exp \left(1 - \frac {c t ^ {2}}{\sigma^ {2}}\right).
$$

Then it follows by union bound that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} (T \geq t) \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\max  _ {j _ {0}, j _ {1}, j _ {2}, j _ {3}} \left| \left\langle \mathcal {Z}, [   [ \mathcal {S} ^ {(j _ {0})}; \mathbf {V} _ {1} ^ {(j _ {1})}, \mathbf {V} _ {2} ^ {(j _ {2})}, \mathbf {V} _ {3} ^ {(j _ {3})} ]   ] \right\rangle \right| \geq \frac {t}{2}\right) \\ \leq N _ {\mathcal {S}} N _ {1} N _ {2} N _ {3} \exp \left(1 - \frac {c t ^ {2}}{4 \sigma^ {2}}\right) \\ \leq \exp \left(1 - \frac {c t ^ {2}}{4 \sigma^ {2}} + C (\varepsilon) \left(r _ {1} r _ {2} r _ {3} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} p _ {k} r _ {k}\right)\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Now by taking  $t = C\sigma \sqrt{r_1r_2r_3 + \sum_{k=1}^{3}p_kr_k}$ , we have with probability at least  $1 - \exp\left(-c(r_1r_2r_3 + \sum_{k=1}^{3}p_kr_k)\right)$ ,

$$
T \leq C \sigma \left(r _ {1} r _ {2} r _ {3} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} p _ {k} r _ {k}\right) ^ {1 / 2},
$$

which has finished the proof of this lemma.  $\square$

Lemma E.6 Suppose  $\mathcal{A}_i$  are Gaussian ensembles with variance  $1 / n$ , i.e., each entry of  $\mathcal{A}_i$  comes from  $N(0,1 / n)$  independent. Let  $\mathcal{A}$  be the affine map such that  $[\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{X})]_i = \langle \mathcal{A}_i,\mathcal{X}\rangle$ , and let  $\mathcal{A}^*$  be the adjoint operator of  $\mathcal{A}$ . Assume  $n > C\left(r_1r_2r_3 + \sum_{k = 1}^{3}p_kr_k\right)$ , then we have for any rank- $(r_1,r_2,r_3)$  tensor  $\mathcal{X}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_1\times p_2\times p_3}$ ,

$$
\frac {9}{1 0} \| \mathcal {X} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq \| \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X}) \| \leq \frac {1 1}{9} \| \mathcal {X} \| _ {\mathrm {F}}, \tag {E.9}
$$

$$
\| \mathcal {A} ^ {*} \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq \frac {6}{5} \left(\sqrt {\frac {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}}{n}} \vee 1\right) \| \mathcal {X} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \tag {E.10}
$$

hold with probability at least  $1 - C \exp \left(-c\left(r_1r_2r_3 + \sum_{k = 1}^{3}p_kr_k\right)\right)$ .

Proof of Lemma E.6. By (Rauhut, Schneider and Stojanac, 2017, Theorem 2), for any  $\delta, \varepsilon \in (0,1)$ , we have

$$
(1 - \delta) \| \mathcal {X} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq \| \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq (1 + \delta) \| \mathcal {X} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}
$$

for arbitrary rank-  $(r_1,r_2,r_3)$  tensors  $\mathcal{X}$  with probability at least  $1 - \varepsilon$  provided that

$$
n \geq C \delta^ {- 2} \max  \left\{\left(r _ {1} r _ {2} r _ {3} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {3} p _ {k} r _ {k}\right), \log (\varepsilon^ {- 1}) \right\}.
$$

Setting  $\delta = 1 / 10$  and  $\varepsilon = \exp \left(-C(r_1r_2r_3 + \sum_{k = 1}^{3}p_kr_k)\right)$ , (E.9) is established. To prove (E.10), we first note that  $\| \mathcal{A}^*\|$  is equivalent to the spectral norm of a (scaled) Gaussian random matrix of size  $n\times (p_1p_2p_3)$ . By random matrix theory (Vershynin, 2010, Corollary 5.35), we have with probability at least  $1 - 2\exp (-cp)$ ,

$$
\left\| \sqrt {n} \mathcal {A} ^ {*} \right\| \leq \sqrt {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}} + \sqrt {n} + \frac {1}{1 1} (\sqrt {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}} \vee \sqrt {n}) \leq \frac {1 2}{1 1} (\sqrt {p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3}} \vee \sqrt {n}),
$$

then (E.10) follows from (E.9) and the fact  $\| \mathcal{A}^*\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{X})\| \leq \| \mathcal{A}^*\| \| \mathcal{A}(\mathcal{X})\|$ .

Lemma E.7 Let  $\mathcal{A}$  be a linear map from  $\mathbb{R}^{p_1\times p_2\times p_3}$  to  $\mathbb{R}^n$  such that for any rank-  $(\boldsymbol {r}_1 + \boldsymbol {r}_2)$  tensor  $\mathcal{X}$

$$
(1 - \delta) \| \mathcal {X} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq \| \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \leq (1 + \delta) \| \mathcal {X} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2},
$$

Then, for all rank- $\pmb{r}_1$  tensor  $\mathcal{X}$  and rank- $\pmb{r}_2$  tensor  $\mathcal{Y}$ , we have

$$
| \langle \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X}), \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {Y}) \rangle - \langle \mathcal {X}, \mathcal {Y} \rangle | \leq \delta \| \mathcal {X} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \| \mathcal {Y} \| _ {\mathrm {F}}.
$$

Proof of Lemma E.7. Without loss of generality we assume  $\| \mathcal{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}} = \| \mathcal{Y}\|_{\mathrm{F}} = 1$ , then the proof simply follows from the application of the parallelogram identity:

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X}), \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {Y}) \rangle - \langle \mathcal {X}, \mathcal {Y} \rangle | \\ = \frac {1}{4} \left| \left(\| \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X} + \mathcal {Y}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} - \| \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X} - \mathcal {Y}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}\right) - \left(\| \mathcal {X} + \mathcal {Y} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} - \| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {Y} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}\right) \right| \\ \leq \frac {1}{4} \left| \| \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X} + \mathcal {Y}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} - \| \mathcal {X} + \mathcal {Y} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \right| + \left| \| \mathcal {A} (\mathcal {X} - \mathcal {Y}) \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} - \| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {Y} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} \right| \\ \leq \frac {\delta}{4} \left(\| \mathcal {X} + \mathcal {Y} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + \| \mathcal {X} - \mathcal {Y} \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2}\right) \leq \delta . \\ \end{array}
$$

Lemma E.8 (Poisson Tail Bound) Suppose  $W \sim \mathrm{Poisson}(v)$ , then for any  $x \geq 0$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(W \geq v + x\right) \leq \exp \left(- \frac {x ^ {2}}{2 v} \psi_ {B e n n} (x / v)\right).
$$

For any  $0 \leq x \leq v$ ,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(W \leq v - x\right) \leq \exp \left(- \frac {x ^ {2}}{2 v} \psi_ {B e n n} (- x / v)\right) \leq \exp \left(- \frac {x ^ {2}}{2 v}\right),
$$

where  $\psi_{Benn}(t) = \frac{(1 + t)\log(1 + t) - t}{t^2 / 2}$  for  $t > -1, t \neq 0$ , and  $\psi_{Benn}(0) = 1$ .

Specifically, for any  $0 < x < 2v$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {P} (W \geq v + x) \leq \exp \left(- \frac {x ^ {2}}{4 v}\right).
$$

Proof of Lemma E.8. See Boucheron, Lugosi and Massart (2013).

![](images/907309e45af22b2d00237f044b77d5b6af8117b595854afcd38488777080bd51.jpg)

Lemma E.9 (Bias and Subgaussian of Truncated Poisson Distribution) Suppose  $W \sim \text{Poisson}(\nu)$  and  $W' = W1\left\{\frac{1}{10}\nu \leq W \leq 10\nu\right\} + \nu 1\left\{W \notin [\frac{1}{10}\nu, 10\nu]\right\}$ , then for any  $\epsilon > 0$ , there exists  $C_{\epsilon} > 0$  that only depends on  $\epsilon$ , such that for all  $\nu \geq C_{\epsilon}$ , we have

$$
\left| \mathbb {E} \log \left(W ^ {\prime} + \frac {1}{2}\right) - \log \nu \right| \leq 4 \nu^ {- \frac {3}{2} + \epsilon},
$$

and we can also find some absolute constant  $C, K_0 > 0$  such that if  $\nu \geq C$ , then

$$
\left| \mathbb {E} \left(\log^ {2} \left(W ^ {\prime} + 1 / 2\right)\right) - \log^ {2} \nu \right| \leq 4 / \nu ,
$$

$$
\left\| \sqrt {\nu} \left(\log \left(W ^ {\prime} + \frac {1}{2}\right) - \mathbb {E} \log \left(W ^ {\prime} + \frac {1}{2}\right)\right) \right\| _ {\psi_ {2}} \leq K _ {0}.
$$

Proof of Lemma E.9. see (Shi, Zhou and Zhang, 2018, Lemma 1, Lemma 2, Lemma 3).

Lemma E.10 Let  $\mathcal{Y}_{ijk} \sim \text{Poisson}(I\exp(\mathcal{X}_{ijk}))$  independently, and each entry of  $\mathcal{X}$  is bounded with  $|\mathcal{X}_{ijk}| \leq B$ . Suppose  $I \geq C\left(Be^{B}\log \overline{p} \vee 1\right)$ , then with probability at least  $1 - \frac{c}{\overline{p}}$ , we have

$$
\sup_{\substack{\mathbf{W}_{l}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{l}\times r_{l}},\| \mathbf{W}_{l}\| \leq 1,l = 1,2,3\\ \mathcal{S}\in \mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\| \mathcal{S}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}}\langle \mathcal{Z},\mathbf{S}\times_{1} \mathbf{W}_{1}\times_{2} \mathbf{W}_{2}\times_{3} \mathbf{W}_{3}\rangle \leq C\sqrt{\frac{df\cdot e^{B}}{I}}.
$$

where  $\mathcal{Z} := \mathcal{Y} / I - \exp(\mathcal{X}^*)$ , and  $df := r_1r_2r_3 + \sum_{k=1}^{3}p_kr_k$ .

Proof of Lemma E.10. For each Poisson random variable  $\mathcal{V}_{jkl}$ , define

$$
\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} = \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} 1 _ {\left\{\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} \leq 3 I \exp \left(\mathcal {X} _ {j k l}\right) \right\}},
$$

and let  $\mathcal{Z}' = \mathcal{Y}' / I - \mathbb{E}\mathcal{Y}' / I$ . Notice that when  $\mathcal{Y} = \mathcal{Y}'$ , we have  $\mathcal{Z} = \mathcal{Z}' + \mathbb{E}(\mathcal{Y}' - \mathcal{Y}) / I$ . Now for any  $t > 0$ , we have

$$
\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\substack{\mathbf{W}_{l}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{l}\times r_{l}},\| \mathbf{W}_{l}\| \leq 1\\ \mathcal{S}\in \mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\| \mathcal{S}\|_{\mathbb{F}}\leq 1}}|\langle \mathcal{Z},\mathcal{S}\times_{1}\mathbf{W}_{1}\times_{2}\mathbf{W}_{2}\times_{3}\mathbf{W}_{3}\rangle | > t\right)
$$

(E.11)

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\substack{\mathbf{W}_{l}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{l}\times r_{l}},\| \mathbf{W}_{l}\| \leq 1\\ \mathcal{S}\in \mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\| \mathcal{S}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}}\big|\big\langle \mathcal{Z}^{\prime} + \mathbb{E}(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime} - \mathcal{Y}) / I,\mathcal{S}\times_{1}\mathbf{W}_{1}\times_{2}\mathbf{W}_{2}\times_{3}\mathbf{W}_{3}\big\rangle \big| > t\right) \\ + \mathbb {P} \left(\exists (j, k, l), \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} \neq \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

We first provide an upper bound for the second probability:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} (\exists (j, k, l), \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} \neq \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l}) = \mathbb {P} \left(\cup_ {j, k, l} \left\{\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} \neq \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} \right\}\right) \\ \leq \sum_ {j, k, l} \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} \neq \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l}\right) = \sum_ {j, k, l} \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} > 3 I \exp \left(\mathcal {X} _ {j k l}\right)\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

(E.12)

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \sum_ {j, k, l} ^ {\text {L e m m a E . 8}} \exp (- I \exp (\mathcal {X} _ {j k l})) \\ \leq p _ {1} p _ {2} p _ {3} \exp (- I e ^ {- B}) \leq c / \bar {p} \\ \end{array}
$$

Here in the last step we use the assumption that  $I > Ce^{B}\log \overline{p}$ . Now we study the first probability in (E.11), first of all, we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathbb {E} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} - \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l}\right) / I \right| = \mathbb {E} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} 1 _ {\left\{\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} > 3 I \exp \left(\mathcal {X} _ {j k l}\right) \right\}}\right) / I \\ \leq \frac {1}{I} \left(\mathbb {E} \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \left(\mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} > 3 I \exp \left(\mathcal {X} _ {j k l}\right)\right)\right) ^ {1 / 2} \\ \end{array}
$$

(E.13) Lemma E.8  $\leq \frac{1}{I}\left(\sqrt{2} I\exp (\mathcal{X}_{jkl})\right)\exp \left(-\frac{1}{2} I\exp (\mathcal{X}_{jkl})\right)$

$$
\leq \sqrt {2} \exp \left(- \frac {1}{2} I e ^ {- B} + B\right) \leq \sqrt {2} \exp \left(\frac {1}{2} B - \frac {1}{2} \log I\right) = \sqrt {\frac {2 e ^ {B}}{I}}.
$$

Next, we claim that  $\{\mathcal{Z}_{jkl}^{\prime}\}$  are independent sub-Gaussian random variables. The following inequality bulids the tail bound of  $\left|\mathcal{Z}_{jkl}^{\prime}\right|$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(| \mathcal {Z} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} | > t\right) = \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {Z} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} > t\right) + \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {Z} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} <   - t\right) \\ = \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} - \mathbb {E} \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} > I t\right) + \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} - \mathbb {E} \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} <   - I t\right) \\ \leq^ {(E. 1 3)} \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} > \mathbb {E} \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} - \sqrt {2 I e ^ {B}} + I t\right) + \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} <   \mathbb {E} \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} - I t\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

The upper tail probability can be bounded when  $t$  takes different values:

-  $2\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}} < t < \sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}} + 2\exp(\mathcal{X}_{jkl})$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} > \mathbb {E} \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} - \sqrt {2 I e ^ {B}} + I t\right) \\ \stackrel {\text {L e m m a E . 8}} {\leq} \exp \left(- \frac {1}{4} \frac {I ^ {2} (t - \sqrt {2 I ^ {- 1} e ^ {B}}) ^ {2}}{I \exp (\mathcal {X} _ {j k l})}\right) \\ \leq \exp \left(- \frac {1}{4} \frac {I (t / 2) ^ {2}}{\exp (\mathcal {X} _ {j k l})}\right) \leq \exp \left(- \frac {I t ^ {2}}{1 6 e ^ {B}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

$t > \sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}} + 2\exp(\chi_{jkl})$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} > \mathbb {E} \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} - \sqrt {2 I e ^ {B}} + I t\right) = 0.
$$

In conclusion, we have

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} > \mathbb {E} \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} - \sqrt {2 I e ^ {B}} + I t\right) \leq \exp \left(- \frac {I t ^ {2}}{1 6 e ^ {B}}\right), \quad \forall t \geq 2 \sqrt {2 I ^ {- 1} e ^ {B}}.
$$

In the meantime, for any  $t > 0$  we have

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} <   \mathbb {E} \mathcal {Y} _ {j k l} - I t\right) \stackrel {\text {L e m m a E . 8}} {\leq} \exp \left(- \frac {I ^ {2} t ^ {2}}{2 I \exp (\mathcal {X} _ {j k l})}\right) \leq \exp \left(- \frac {I t ^ {2}}{2 e ^ {B}}\right).
$$

Thus

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left| \mathcal {Z} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} \right| > t\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {I t ^ {2}}{1 6 e ^ {B}}\right), \quad \forall t \geq 2 \sqrt {2 I ^ {- 1} e ^ {B}}.
$$

Now for any  $q\geq 1$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left| \mathcal {Z} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {q} = \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \mathcal {Z} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} \right| > x\right) q x ^ {q - 1} d x \\ \leq \int_ {0} ^ {2 \sqrt {2 I ^ {- 1} e ^ {B}}} \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \mathcal {Z} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} \right| > x\right) q x ^ {q - 1} d x + \int_ {2 \sqrt {2 I ^ {- 1} e ^ {B}}} ^ {\infty} 2 \exp \left(- \frac {I x ^ {2}}{1 6 e ^ {B}}\right) q x ^ {q - 1} d x \\ \leq \int_ {0} ^ {2 \sqrt {2 I ^ {- 1} e ^ {B}}} q x ^ {q - 1} d x + \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} 2 \exp \left(- \frac {I x ^ {2}}{1 6 e ^ {B}}\right) q x ^ {q - 1} d x \\ = \left(2 \sqrt {2 I ^ {- 1} e ^ {B}}\right) ^ {q} + 4 q \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} e ^ {- y} \left(\frac {4 e ^ {B / 2} \sqrt {y}}{\sqrt {I}}\right) ^ {q - 1} \frac {e ^ {B / 2}}{\sqrt {I y}} d y \\ = \left(2 \sqrt {2 I ^ {- 1} e ^ {B}}\right) ^ {q} + 4 ^ {q} q e ^ {q B / 2} I ^ {- q / 2} \Gamma (q / 2) \\ \leq \left(2 \sqrt {2 I ^ {- 1} e ^ {B}}\right) ^ {q} + 4 ^ {q} q e ^ {q B / 2} I ^ {- q / 2} \left(\frac {q}{2}\right) ^ {q / 2} \\ \leq \left(2 \sqrt {2 I ^ {- 1} e ^ {B}} + 2 \sqrt {2} q ^ {1 / q} \sqrt {\frac {q e ^ {B}}{I}}\right) ^ {q}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Here the last inequality comes from the fact that  $x^{q} + y^{q} \leq (x + y)^{q}$  for  $q \geq 1$ . Then we can bound the  $\psi_{2}$  norm of  $\mathcal{Z}_{jkl}'$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathcal {Z} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {\psi_ {2}} = \sup  _ {q \geq 1} p ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(\mathbb {E} | \mathcal {Z} _ {j k l} ^ {\prime} | ^ {q}\right) ^ {1 / p} \leq 2 \sqrt {2} \sup  _ {q \geq 1} q ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(\sqrt {I ^ {- 1} e ^ {B}} + q ^ {1 / q} \sqrt {\frac {q e ^ {B}}{I}}\right) \\ \leq 2 \sqrt {2} \left(\sqrt {\frac {e ^ {B}}{I}} + e ^ {\frac {1}{e}} \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {B}}{I}}\right) \leq C \sqrt {\frac {e ^ {B}}{I}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Now by Lemma E.5, we have some universal constant  $C$ , such that with probability  $1 - e^{c \cdot df}$

$$
\sup_{\substack{\mathbf{W}_{l}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{l}\times r_{l}},\| \mathbf{W}_{l}\| \leq 1\\ \mathcal{S}\in \mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\| \mathcal{S}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}}\left\langle \mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}\times_{1}\mathbf{W}_{1}\times_{2}\mathbf{W}_{2}\times_{3}\mathbf{W}_{3}\right\rangle \leq C\sqrt{\frac{df\cdot e^{B}}{I}}. \tag{E.14}
$$

Combining (E.13) and (E.14), we have

(E.15)

$$
\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\substack{\mathbf{W}_{l}\in \mathbb{R}^{p_{l}\times r_{l}},\| \mathbf{W}_{l}\| \leq 1\\ \mathcal{S}\in \mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\| \mathcal{S}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1}}\big|\big\langle \mathcal{Z}^{\prime} + \mathbb{E}(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime} - \mathcal{Y}) / I,[[\mathcal{S};\mathbf{W}_{1},\mathbf{W}_{2},\mathbf{W}_{3}]]\big\rangle \big| > (C + 1)\sqrt{\frac{df\cdot e^{B}}{I}}\right)\leq e^{c\cdot df},
$$

and the conclusion follows by combining (E.11), (E.12) and (E.15).  $\square$

Lemma E.11 Suppose  $Y \sim \text{Binomial}(N, s(x))$ , where  $s(x) = 1 / (1 + e^{-x})$ . Let  $\hat{p}' = \hat{p} 1_{\{| \hat{p} - p | \leq \frac{p}{2} \}} + p 1_{\{| \hat{p} - p | > \frac{p}{2} \}}$  and  $X = \log \left( \frac{\hat{p}' + \frac{1}{2N}}{1 - \hat{p}' + \frac{1}{2N}} \right)$  where  $p = s(x)$ ,  $\hat{p} = Y / N$ . Then if  $N \geq C | x | e^{3|x|}$ , for any  $\epsilon > 0$ , we have universal constants  $K_0, C$ , such that

$$
| \mathbb {E} X - x | \leq C e ^ {3 | x |} N ^ {- \frac {3}{2} + \varepsilon},
$$

(E.16)

$$
\left\| e ^ {- | x |} \sqrt {N} (X - \mathbb {E} X) \right\| _ {\phi_ {2}} \leq K _ {0}.
$$

Proof of Lemma E.11: To simplify the proof, we only consider the case where  $x \leq 0$ . Let  $g(t) = \log \left(\frac{t + \frac{1}{2N}}{1 - t + \frac{1}{2N}}\right), t \in [0,1]$ , then we have  $X = g(\hat{p}^{\prime})$ . We first provide the derivatives of  $g(t)$  up to order 3:

$$
g ^ {\prime} (t) = \left(t + \frac {1}{2 N}\right) ^ {- 1} + \left(1 - t + \frac {1}{2 N}\right) ^ {- 1},
$$

(E.17)

$$
g ^ {\prime \prime \prime} (t) = 2 \left(t + \frac {1}{2 N}\right) ^ {- 3} + 2 \left(1 - t + \frac {1}{2 N}\right) ^ {- 3}.
$$

Let  $p \coloneqq s(x) \leq \frac{1}{2}$ , by Taylor's expansion of  $g(t)$  at  $t_0 = \frac{N + 1}{N} p - \frac{1}{2N}$ , we have

(E.18)  $g(t) = g(t_0) + g'(t_0)(t - t_0) + \frac{1}{2} g''(t_0)(t - t_0)^2 +\frac{1}{6} g'''(\xi)(\xi -t_0)^3,$

where  $\xi$  is some number between  $t_0$  and  $t$ . Now we let  $f(t) = g(t) - g(t_0) - g'(t_0)(t - t_0) - \frac{1}{2} g''(t_0)(t - t_0)^2$ , then one can see that as long as  $-\frac{1}{2N} \leq t_0 - N^{-\frac{1}{2} + \epsilon} \leq t \leq t_0 + N^{-\frac{1}{2} + \epsilon} \leq 1 + \frac{1}{2N}$  (the first and third inequality holds since  $N > C|x|e^{3|x|} > Cp^{-3}$ ), we have some universal constant  $C$ , such that

$|f(t)| \leq \frac{1}{3} \sup_{t: |t - t_0| \leq N^{-\frac{1}{2} + \epsilon}} \left( \left| t + \frac{1}{2N} \right|^{-3} + \left| 1 - t + \frac{1}{2N} \right|^{-3} \right) |t - t_0|^3$  (E.19)

$$
\leq \frac {2}{3} \left| \frac {N + 1}{N} p - N ^ {- \frac {1}{2} + \epsilon} \right| ^ {- 3} N ^ {- 3 / 2 + 3 \epsilon} \leq C p ^ {- 3} N ^ {- \frac {3}{2} + \epsilon_ {1}}
$$

with  $\epsilon_{1} = 3\epsilon$ . Here the last inequality comes from the following fact:

$$
\frac {N + 1}{N} p - N ^ {- \frac {1}{2} + \epsilon} \geq \frac {N + 1}{N} p - p ^ {\frac {3}{2} - 3 \epsilon} \geq c p.
$$

Thus it follows that

(E.20)  $\mathbb{E}\left|f(\hat{p})1_{\{t_0 - N^{-\frac{1}{2} +\epsilon} <   \hat{p} <  t_0 - N^{-\frac{1}{2} +\epsilon}\}}\right|\leq Cp^{-3}N^{-\frac{3}{2} +\epsilon_1} = C(e^{|x|} + 1)^3 N^{-\frac{3}{2} +\epsilon_1}.$

Then we have

(E.21)

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} | f (\hat {p}) | \leq \mathbb {E} \left| f (\hat {p}) 1 _ {\{t _ {0} - N ^ {- \frac {1}{2} + \epsilon} <   \hat {p} <   t _ {0} - N ^ {- \frac {1}{2} + \epsilon} \}} \right| + \mathbb {E} \left| f (\hat {p}) 1 _ {\{\hat {p} \in [ 0, t _ {0} - N ^ {- \frac {1}{2} + \epsilon} ] \cup [ t _ {0} + N ^ {- \frac {1}{2} + \epsilon}, 1 ] \}} \right| \\ \leq C \left(e ^ {| x |} + 1\right) ^ {3} N ^ {- \frac {3}{2} + \epsilon_ {1}} + \sup  _ {t \in [ 0, 1 ]} | g ^ {\prime \prime \prime} (t) | \cdot \mathbb {P} \left(| \hat {p} - t _ {0} | > N ^ {- \frac {1}{2} + \epsilon}\right) \\ \leq C \left(e ^ {| x |} + 1\right) ^ {3} N ^ {- \frac {3}{2} + \epsilon_ {1}} + \frac {3 2}{3} N ^ {3} \mathbb {P} \left(| \hat {p} - p | > N ^ {- \frac {1}{2} + \epsilon} - N ^ {- 1}\right) \\ \leq C \left(e ^ {| x |} + 1\right) ^ {3} N ^ {- \frac {3}{2} + \epsilon_ {1}} + \frac {3 2}{3} N ^ {3} \mathbb {P} \left(| \hat {p} - p | > \frac {1}{2} N ^ {- \frac {1}{2} + \epsilon}\right) \\ \leq C \left(e ^ {| x |} + 1\right) ^ {3} N ^ {- \frac {3}{2} + \epsilon_ {1}} + C N ^ {3} e ^ {- \frac {1}{2} N ^ {2 \varepsilon}} \leq C e ^ {3 | x |} N ^ {- \frac {3}{2} + \epsilon_ {1}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last but one inequality comes from applying the following concentration inequality for Binomial random variable:

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(| Y - p | \geq t\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- 2 N t ^ {2}\right), \quad \forall t \geq 0. \tag {E.22}
$$

In the mean time, one can calculate that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p}) - \mathbb {E} f (\hat {p}) = g (t _ {0}) + \frac {2 p - 1}{(N + 1) ^ {2} p (1 - p)} \left(\frac {1}{8 p (1 - p)} - \frac {1}{2}\right) \tag {E.23} \\ = \log (\frac {p}{1 - p}) + \frac {2 p - 1}{(N + 1) ^ {2} p (1 - p)} \left(\frac {1}{8 p (1 - p)} - \frac {1}{2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Combining (E.21) and (E.23), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p}) - x | = \left| \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p}) - \log \left(\frac {p}{1 - p}\right) - \mathbb {E} f (\hat {p}) + \mathbb {E} f (\hat {p}) \right| \\ \leq \left| \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p}) - \log \left(\frac {p}{1 - p}\right) - \mathbb {E} f (\hat {p}) \right| + | \mathbb {E} f (\hat {p}) | \\ \leq \frac {2 p - 1}{(N + 1) ^ {2} p (1 - p)} \left(\frac {1}{8 p (1 - p)} - \frac {1}{2}\right) + C e ^ {3 | x |} N ^ {- \frac {3}{2} + \epsilon_ {1}} \\ \leq C e ^ {2 | x |} N ^ {- 2} + C e ^ {3 | x |} N ^ {- \frac {3}{2} + \epsilon_ {1}} \\ \leq C e ^ {3 | x |} N ^ {- \frac {3}{2} + \epsilon_ {1}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Now we calculate the bias of  $X = g(\hat{p}^{\prime})$ . First,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathbb {E} g \left(\hat {p} ^ {\prime}\right) - \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p}) \right| = \left| g (p) \mathbb {P} \left(| \hat {p} - p | \geq \frac {p}{2}\right) + \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p}) 1 _ {\{| \hat {p} - p | \leq \frac {p}{2} \}} - \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p}) \right| \\ \leq \left| g (p) \mathbb {P} (\hat {p} \leq \frac {p}{2}) - \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p}) 1 _ {\{\hat {p} \leq \frac {p}{2} \}} \right| + \left| g (p) \mathbb {P} (\hat {p} \geq \frac {3 p}{2}) - \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p}) 1 _ {\{\hat {p} \geq \frac {3 p}{2} \}} \right| \\ \leq \max \left\{g (p) \mathbb {P} (\hat {p} \leq \frac {p}{2}), \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p}) 1 _ {\{\hat {p} \leq \frac {p}{2} \}} \right\} + \max \left\{g (p) \mathbb {P} (\hat {p} \geq \frac {3 p}{2}), \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p}) 1 _ {\{\hat {p} \geq \frac {3 p}{2} \}} \right\} \\ \stackrel {(a)} {\leq} \max  \left\{g (p) e ^ {- \frac {1}{2} N p ^ {2}}, \left(\mathbb {E} g ^ {2} (\hat {p})\right) ^ {1 / 2} \left(\mathbb {P} (\hat {p} \leq \frac {p}{2})\right) ^ {1 / 2} \right\} \\ + \max  \left\{g (p) e ^ {- \frac {1}{2} N p ^ {2}}, \left(\mathbb {E} g ^ {2} (\hat {p})\right) ^ {1 / 2} \left(\mathbb {P} (\hat {p} \geq \frac {3 p}{2})\right) ^ {1 / 2} \right\} \\ \leq 2 \max  \left\{g (p) e ^ {- \frac {1}{2} N p ^ {2}}, \left(\mathbb {E} g ^ {2} (\hat {p})\right) ^ {1 / 2} e ^ {- \frac {1}{2} N p ^ {2}} \right\} \\ \stackrel {(b)} {\leq} 2 \log (2 N + 1) e ^ {- \frac {1}{2} N p ^ {2}} \leq 2 \log (2 N + 1) e ^ {- \frac {1}{2} N ^ {1 / 3}} \leq C e ^ {3 | x |} N ^ {- \frac {3}{2} + \epsilon_ {1}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Here, we use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality for (a), and apply the uniform bound  $g(p) \leq \log(2N + 1)$  for (b). Combining two inequalities above, we obtain

$$
\left| \mathbb {E} g \left(\hat {p} ^ {\prime}\right) - x \right| \leq \left| \mathbb {E} g \left(p ^ {\prime}\right) - \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p}) \right| + \left| \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p}) - x \right| \leq C e ^ {3 | x |} N ^ {- \frac {3}{2} + \epsilon_ {1}},
$$

which gives the first inequality of (E.16).

Now we prove the second inequality of (E.16). We denote  $D = g(\hat{p}^{\prime}) - \mathbb{E}g(\hat{p}^{\prime})$  for convenience. By what we have proved, we have  $|\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p}^{\prime}) - x| \leq Ce^{3|x|}N^{-\frac{3}{2} +\epsilon_1} \leq Ce^{2|x|}N^{-1}$ , thus we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\left| e ^ {x} \sqrt {N} D \right| > t\right) = \mathbb {P} \left(e ^ {x} \sqrt {N} D > t\right) + \mathbb {P} \left(e ^ {x} \sqrt {N} D <   - t\right) \\ = \mathbb {P} \left(g \left(\hat {p} ^ {\prime}\right) <   \mathbb {E} g \left(\hat {p} ^ {\prime}\right) - \frac {t e ^ {- x}}{\sqrt {N}}\right) + \mathbb {P} \left(g \left(\hat {p} ^ {\prime}\right) > \mathbb {E} g \left(\hat {p} ^ {\prime}\right) + \frac {t e ^ {- x}}{\sqrt {N}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Then

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(g (\hat {p} ^ {\prime}) <   \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p} ^ {\prime}) - \frac {t e ^ {- x}}{\sqrt {N}}\right) \leq \mathbb {P} \left(g (\hat {p} ^ {\prime}) <   x - t e ^ {- x} N ^ {- 1 / 2} + C e ^ {- 2 x} N ^ {- 1}\right),
$$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(g (\hat {p} ^ {\prime}) > \mathbb {E} g (\hat {p} ^ {\prime}) + \frac {t e ^ {- x}}{\sqrt {N}}\right) \leq \mathbb {P} \left(g (\hat {p} ^ {\prime}) > x + t e ^ {- x} N ^ {- 1 / 2} - C e ^ {- 2 x} N ^ {- 1}\right).
$$

We first investigate the lower tail bound:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(g \left(\hat {p} ^ {\prime}\right) <   x - t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2} + C e ^ {2 | x |} N ^ {- 1}\right) \\ = \mathbb {P} \left(\hat {p} ^ {\prime} <   \frac {1 + 1 / N}{\exp \left(- x + t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2} - C e ^ {2 | x |} N ^ {- 1}\right) + 1} - \frac {1}{2 N}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

When  $t > 1$ , since  $te^{|x|}N^{-1/2} - Ce^{2|x|}N^{-1} > \frac{1}{2}te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}$  by the assumption on  $N$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\hat {p} <   \frac {1 + 1 / N}{\exp \left(- x + t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2} - C e ^ {2 | x |} N ^ {- 1}\right) + 1} - \frac {1}{2 N}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\hat {p} <   \frac {1 + 1 / N}{\exp \left(- x + \frac {1}{2} t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) + 1} - \frac {1}{2 N}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

When  $t > 6e^{x}N^{1 / 2}$  , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(p ^ {\prime} <   \frac {1 + 1 / N}{\exp \left(- x + \frac {1}{2} t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) + 1} - \frac {1}{2 N}\right) \leq \mathbb {P} \left(p ^ {\prime} <   \frac {1 . 1}{\exp \left(- x + \frac {1}{2} t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) + 1}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(p ^ {\prime} \leq \frac {1 . 1}{\exp (- x + 3) + 1}\right) = \mathbb {P} \left(p ^ {\prime} \leq \frac {1 . 1}{e ^ {3} \exp (- x) + 1}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(p ^ {\prime} \leq \frac {1}{2 (\exp (- x) + 1)}\right) = \mathbb {P} \left(p ^ {\prime} \leq \frac {p}{2}\right) = 0, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last identity comes from the definition of  $p'$ . When  $1 < t < 6e^{x}N^{1/2}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\hat {p} <   \frac {1 + 1 / N}{\exp \left(- x + \frac {1}{2} t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) + 1} - \frac {1}{2 N}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\hat {p} <   \frac {1 + 1 / N}{\exp \left(- x + \frac {1}{2} t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) + 1}\right) \\ = \mathbb {P} \left(\hat {p} - p <   \frac {1 + 1 / N}{\exp \left(- x + \frac {1}{2} t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) + 1} - \frac {1}{1 + e ^ {- x}}\right) \\ = \mathbb {P} \left(\hat {p} - p <   \frac {e ^ {- x} \left(1 - e ^ {\frac {1}{2} t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2}}\right) + N ^ {- 1} \left(1 + e ^ {- x}\right)}{\left(\exp \left(- x + \frac {1}{2} t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) + 1\right) \left(1 + e ^ {- x}\right)}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\hat {p} - p <   \frac {- \frac {1}{2} \left(e ^ {\frac {1}{2} t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2}} - 1\right) + N ^ {- 1}}{\exp \left(- x + \frac {1}{2} t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) + 1}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\hat {p} - p <   - \frac {1}{4} \frac {t e ^ {| x |} N ^ {- 1 / 2}}{\exp (- x + 3) + 1} + \frac {1}{N (e ^ {- x} + 1)}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\hat {p} - p <   - \frac {1}{4 e ^ {3}} t N ^ {- 1 / 2} + \frac {1}{N ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(e ^ {- x} + 1\right)} N ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\hat {p} - p <   - \frac {1}{4 e ^ {3}} t N ^ {- 1 / 2} + c t N ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \leq \exp \left(- c t ^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Thus we have proved that  $\forall t > 1$ , there exists constant  $c$ , such that the lower tail bound

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(e ^ {x} \sqrt {N} D <   - t\right) \leq e ^ {- c t ^ {2}}.
$$

We can prove the similar result for upper tail bound. Thus,  $\forall p\geq 1$  , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left| e ^ {x} \sqrt {N} D \right| ^ {q} = \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} \mathbb {P} \left(\left| e ^ {x} \sqrt {N} D \right| > t\right) q t ^ {q - 1} d t \leq \int_ {0} ^ {1} q t ^ {q - 1} d t + \int_ {1} ^ {\infty} e ^ {- c t ^ {2}} q t ^ {q - 1} d t \\ \leq \int_ {0} ^ {1} q t ^ {q - 1} d t + \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} e ^ {- c t ^ {2}} q t ^ {q - 1} d t = 1 + c ^ {- \frac {q}{2}} q \Gamma \left(\frac {q}{2}\right) \\ \leq 1 + q c ^ {- \frac {q}{2}} \left(\frac {q}{2}\right) ^ {\frac {q}{2}} \leq \left(1 + q ^ {\frac {1}{q}} c ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(\frac {q}{2}\right) ^ {1 / 2}\right) ^ {q}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use  $x^q + y^q \leq (x + y)^q$  for all  $x, y \geq 0, q \geq 1$ . Then it follows that

$$
\sup  _ {q \geq 1} q ^ {- \frac {1}{2}} \left(\mathbb {E} \left| e ^ {x} \sqrt {N} D \right| ^ {q}\right) ^ {1 / q} \leq q ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(1 + q ^ {\frac {1}{q}} c ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(\frac {q}{2}\right) ^ {1 / 2}\right) \leq (1 + \frac {e ^ {1 / e} c ^ {- 1 / 2}}{\sqrt {2}}) =: K _ {0},
$$

and we have proved the second inequality of (E.16).
